Speed first | done by the main session, at the owner's request, as far as the "Still open" list in the package: the load from spawn settles about eight times sooner in about a tenth of the memory, and flight keeps the visible world loaded with a few late frames in a hundred. What is open is tuning and the serial stages left on the main thread |
| A. A crust that varies | built and checked; the owner keeps the masses, so the second build is not built. Its cost is package P's to remove |
| B. Caves that open downward, ways back | its first piece, the repair, is built and checked: a third way to paint, `api.replace` (`Turtle.replace`, `Cover.intoSolid`), takes only cells the ground generated as solid, and the cave carve uses it for floors, ceilings and formations (a carve choice is kept, painted or dressed), so none of them can hang in the open where a chamber meets the flank of a rock mass; hollows, treads and the standing stones are painted as before. Law: `structure/inlay`, on a rig the caves plugin registers (of a box that straddles flat ground, the 48 cells under the ground take the stone and none above it does). Both digest lines are unchanged, so no skin was painted into the open in either sampled place; `check-repaint` is clean on planet, caves, city, backrooms and vaults and `check-forget` on lattice; `bench-gen` and `bench-drive` read the same before and after, three runs each. Not looked at: a render of a chamber at a flank, which nobody has found yet. Built since: breaches, drains, wells that go down to the rock's end, and passages between open chambers of different trees (2026-11-18). The census is built (2026-11-18): `caapi-headless cave-census` (of the `--worldgen` world) generates around the root, takes the air cells between half an edge and 1.9 edges under the root's ground, joins them across chunk borders (`hopCell`) and prints how many connected spaces of 64 cells or more there are, their cells, the largest and its share, and the pockets under 64 (`countCaves` in `app/caves.d`). Within 2.5 units of the root: the caves world has 12 spaces, the largest 95 percent of the air (49 percent with `cross-odds` set to 0, so the passages between open chambers of different trees are what joined it); the default world has 8, the largest 57 percent (60 with no cross links: near spawn there are few open trees to join). Law: `structure/cavenetwork` (within 2.5 units of the caves world's root the largest space holds more than four fifths of the cave air; 0.90 measured, with the game's own generation). Around where the caves world starts, at a sinkhole a few edges from the root, it is two systems: the largest holds 0.49 of the air over 8 spaces, so the law is stated for the root and not for everywhere. Landings, in their simplest form (2026-11-18): a drain lays the same deck a well that ends at the rock's end lays (`landing` in `caves.d`: 12 cells across, in the drain's stair wood, one cell above the open vertex its chute or stair comes down to; the first version had a material field and a `fill` of its own, merged into the well's function since); it is painted by the drain's own edge tile in the drain's frame, so it needs no predicate between the caves and the lattice, and it sits under the stair's foot, so a body on it can climb back up. `check-repaint` on the planet is clean and `content-check` finds nothing; the planet's digest lines and `bench-gen` (brushes 341333 as before, parameters 1596 to 1644 ms against 1584 to 1642 the same hour) did not move, which says no drain is painted within 2.5 units of spawn, so no landing has been seen. A law was tried with the landing in a material of its own: from the first place under the default world where the rock has ended, `walk.ensureNearest` found none within 3 rings, and 8 rings ran past the 120 s limit without finding one. So drains under the default world are rare, which follows from the code (inferred, not counted): a drain needs an open chamber right above where the rock ends, and under wild ground a chamber is open only if its tree reaches a sinkhole. The law and the material were taken out (a material needs a use or `content-check` refuses it), and the landing is spruce wood again. The count is built: `cave-census` ends with the open cells below the rock's underside and the cells of spruce wood among them, which there are drain stairs and landings (trees stand on the surface and the lattice's steps are planks; that nothing else lays spruce under the underside is inferred from the content read, not checked against every mod). Out to 4 units from spawn: 48.3 million open cells and 975 of spruce, where one landing alone is 144, so a handful of drains in all. The way down through the caves exists, but too rarely to be found. Making it common is a choice between the owner's "not exactly one way out, but not too obvious either" and finding any way at all: raising `drain-odds` (0.5 already) can at most double them. What limits them, read in `drain` and `keeps` and not counted: the edge must be vertical within half a cell (`VERTICAL_SLACK`), its upper vertex kept (at least `ROCK_MARGIN`, half an edge, inside the rock) and open (a hall, from 1.95 edges down, or a tree reaching a sinkhole), and its lower vertex past the underside, so the underside has to fall within the last half of that one edge. A census of vertical edges by depth would say which of these is the binding one. Not changed: that is the next decision in B. Counted since, at the owner's word to count and then make drains common: `cave-census` now bins the vertical edges by the depth of their upper end and counts those whose upper end is half an edge or more inside the rock and whose lower end is past the underside, the geometry a drain needs; and the spruce it counts is the spruce deeper than 1.5 edges under the ground, because a drain's stair and landing lie mostly in the rock above the underside, so the count below the underside above missed most of them, and the failed law's search had the same fault (its landing then filled, and fill does not paint into rock). Out to 4 units, as node sightings (an edge can be counted from more than one node): 1546 drain-shaped edges, most with the upper end 4 to 5 edges down; 1782 cells of deep spruce, square wells included, so at most a dozen drains. The geometry is not what binds: the upper chamber has to be open, a hall (0.3) or a tree that reaches a sinkhole, and `drain-odds` halves that again. Tried: a kept chamber whose vertical edge down ends past the underside is open as a hall whatever its roll (decision 3's "the lowest chambers open through the crust's floor"). Deep spruce rose to 5683 cells, about three times the drains, and the deep band's largest connected space from 8 to 14 percent of the cave air; but `bench-gen` parameters read 1619, 1619 and 1695 ms against 1577, 1595 and 1611 without it the same hour, with 1.3 percent more brushes (the chambers it opens are painted), and one `bench-drive` run each 1449 against 1376 ms. By the rule that a slowdown is a failure it was taken out at first; asked, the owner said to accept small costs for content asked for, so it is in (`isOverVoid` in `caves.d`, asked by `isHall` only after the hall's roll fails). The planet's digest lines with it: `digest populated=10140 materials 13e92b2fc193bf09a3ab1a359b4e24d7 occupancy 15a86006d3615a1456a326dceba5ba83`, and the `deep` line as it was (the column under spawn holds none of the chambers it opens). 169 tests pass and `check-repaint` is clean on planet, caves and vaults. Long galleries, the owner's pick for joining the trees, the same day: a straight line of edges whose line hash rolls under `gallery-odds` (0.15 in the caves' settings) is a gallery, and the caves' edge tile carves each of its edges as a tunnel (`gallery` in `caves.d`; a rough octagon six cells across, by `replace`, so only rock is taken) wherever the edge is gentle (its ends differ by less than half an edge), both ends are deeper than `HALL_DEPTH` and both lie `ROCK_MARGIN` inside a ground's crust; a ground with no crust (the caves world) gets none, so that world is as it was (digest and tile count). Every open chamber the line passes is joined to it, whichever tree it is in, and a hyperbolic line leaves the band within a few edges, so galleries come out several edges long. Out to 4 units the deep band's largest connected space went from 14 to 21 percent of the cave air (from 8 before the drains), with 280 more separate spaces, galleries that meet no chamber; at 0.3 it was 22 percent, so the odds are not what limits it (taken to be the void windows between the rock masses, which no passage crosses; not shown). `bench-gen` parameters 1632 to 1671 ms against 1619 to 1695 with the drains alone. Planet digest lines with both: `digest populated=10140 materials f273bc16c46ee8ffc1a23b413b1f8803 occupancy 3a09c82b78f57e0ed1af707c667f28ec`, `deep populated=856 materials 635c8ec1184d897a8c0595d17f57d089 occupancy bbd0a787fb263457abdb61824790ab5f`. 169 tests, repaint on planet, caves and vaults clean. The law, at the owner's word to recompute the roll in the test: `structure/gallery` walks the edges of the nodes near a place deep in the rock under spawn, takes those whose line rolls a gallery (the test repeats `hashed`, `unitOf`, `GALLERY_SALT` and `gallery-odds` from the caves, a coupling the owner accepted) and that are gentle with both ends deep inside the crust, and reads the cell at the middle of each edge in the edge's own frame: 20 such edges, all 20 hollow. With `gallery-odds` set to 0 for one run the same 20 edges were 4 hollow (caves that cross them), so the law fails without galleries. 171 tests pass. Mouths, the same day (design 2's third kind of opening): a gentle edge (ends within half an edge in height) whose one end is an open chamber and whose other end lies past the underside rolls a mouth by its edge hash (`mouth-odds` 0.5 in the caves' settings), carved as the same tunnel as a gallery (`tunnel` in `caves.d`, by `replace`, so it takes rock only and ends exactly where the rock ends, opening on the face of the rock mass). Out to 4 units: 81700 more cells of deep cave air and 18700 more open cells below the underside (tunnels through rock past it). The caves world is as it was (no crust, so nothing is ever past the underside); the planet's digest lines with them: `digest populated=10140 materials 5aff259409460bd23902dccff53fb82f occupancy 516a25ff1ca2c146e76ff31cb0a24854`, `deep populated=856 materials 8f7367b72be5a55f78a28df292a7f22f occupancy e01059fa4573c4c714906a379d4f0c91`. `bench-gen` parameters 1698 to 1726 ms against 1635 to 1648 before them, about 3 to 4 percent, from asking the chamber end's openness of every candidate edge; kept under the owner's word on small costs for content asked for. 169 tests, repaint on planet and vaults clean. Not built: the forced route and its laws (a route down a drain from the root's sinkhole network). Tried for the law, the same day: from the first place under the default world where the rock has ended, `walk.ensureNearest` for the drain's and well's spruce found none within 3 rings (0.2 s) or 6 (0.7 s), where the census counts on the order of a hundred drains out to 4 units. The law was taken out. The likelier reason, from the census and not checked: within 2.5 units of spawn the census finds only 405 cells of deep spruce, two or three landings with the square wells, so most drains lie farther out, and the rings of that search hop at one depth from one start. A search that knows where drains are does better: drains and wells lie on the vertical lines under ground vertices, and the deck sits at the first vertex past the underside. Built so: `structure/waydown` walks ground vertices out from spawn (as `walk.ensurePort` does), works out under each how far down the rock ends, and counts the stair wood in the deck's 12 by 12 square at the first vertex past it; the law holds when half the square is wood (the lattice may lay a platform over part of a deck from another edge's task). Measured: 137 vertices searched, 127 of 144 cells, the rock ending 2.008 edges down there. That is within a hundredth of an edge of the wild port whose deck layer read 144 cells of grey brick before the lattice's platforms were made to fill, so it is most likely the same well, and the law holds there because of that fix. 170 tests pass. Not built of the forced route: route stops laid down a way, which would let a script walk it. Tried at the owner's word to build it (2026-11-18), and taken out again, with these findings for the next try:
- A drain's spiral stair is painted in the frame its edge tile was handed, whose roll world code cannot reproduce, so stops laid down it would miss the treads; a chute drain and its deck are square and the same in any roll, so a route goes down a chute.
- The default world already has a route (the landmarks' climbs, 252 waypoints), and stops the world structure lays come before the landmarks' (placed structures lay theirs as the structure grows), so a law has to pick the forced route's stops out, for example as the stops under the ground.
- The planet's sinkhole is cut up to the real surface, so a stop on its rim at the plane's height can be in the air.
- The spawn sinkhole's tree, searched breadth first down its tree links (`isTreeLinked`, the tile's own test), reached 512 chambers down to 5.18 edges and held no chute drain: under spawn the rock ends deeper than chambers are kept (5.2 edges), so no tree there can reach the void.
- A search over 60 surface vertices out from spawn, keeping those where the rock ends within 4.5 edges and trying the descent from each sinkhole entry found there with that sinkhole forced, found none. Which of its filters rejected every candidate was found since, by counting them: every `probeCrust` answered infinity, because the planet gives the root its crust in `sink`, which runs after `mouthsOf`, so while the world structure searches for sinkholes the root has no crust. That is a fault in its own right: `caves.entryNear`, which picks the spawn sinkhole and the landmarks' sinkholes, judges their caves as if the rock never ended. With the root's crust, thin crust and bottom set first in `world`, the same 60 vertices gave 54 where the rock ends within 4.5 edges, 46 sinkhole entries, and 24 of those reach a chute drain down their tree, so the forced route can be found. But setting the crust first broke five tests: the three `live/` sessions stop on "paints ground that is already built" (a ground's tile, tag 93d3d833), `structure/treetrunk` finds no tree and `structure/maze` no maze where the default world hangs it, so the crust is read while the world opens its tree and its maze and the order matters there in a way not traced. It was set back. The route laid at the first of the 24 could not be walked either: a tube's stop lies on its axis, a radius above its floor, so a walking body never comes within the walker's reach of it, and the route has to put its stops at each tube's floor (the radius and bulge of `tubeTo`). Both are the next steps; the search and the route were taken out again. A third round, the same day: run in `sink` after the root's crust, thin crust and bottom (moved to the top of `sink`, which changes nothing generated: the digest lines are as they were), and before the edge tile is registered with its copy of the root, the search lays a route of 9 stops under the ground and leaves the tree, the maze and the `live/` sessions as they were, so the order there is safe. But a body let go at the stop meant to be the chute's top did not move at all, stood on nothing with basalt (the lattice's joints) below it, 2.25 edges down where the rock ends at 2.01: so stops laid by world code with `along(vertex.frame, way)` are not where the cave tile's own frames put that vertex, and a stop's frame has to be checked against the painted cells before a route is laid with them. Taken out again; the reorder of `sink` stays. A fourth round settled why, as far as reading and measuring go: the stops were the forced route's (with the search off the route has none under the ground), the planet's flatten of the routed sinkhole changed nothing about them (the same depth to five places without it), and requiring the drain's chamber to be kept in rock and off any well's line chose the same chamber again. So when the world structure runs, the search judges that chamber at least half an edge inside the rock, and the generated world puts the same spot 0.24 edges below the underside: the world-start queries of the crust disagree with the final ones by about three quarters of an edge there. Taken to be the relief (the underside swings with relief, and relief near a risen ground is scaled down once that ground is found, which happens as the graph grows, after the world structure), not shown. If so, any judgement of the caves made at world start can be off, `entryNear`'s included, and the forced route has to be laid later, by a structure the world places that runs as the graph grows (`place`), not by the world structure itself. That is a design change and the next step. Built so, at the owner's word, the same day: the planet places a structure two edges from spawn (`forcedRoute` in the planet plugin) that runs as the graph grows and searches up to 300 ground vertices out from there, where the rock ends within 4.5 edges, for a sinkhole that opens by its own roll (`probeSunk`; a placed structure cannot add to the root the edge tile was given, so it cannot force one) whose cave tree, searched breadth first down its tree links, reaches a chute drain whose chamber is kept in the rock and off any well's line (`routeToDrain` and `hasChuteBelow` in `caves.d`); there it lays the route: a stop in the sinkhole's bottom chamber, three in each tube down the tree, one at the chute's top and one on its deck. Law: `structure/forcedroute` takes the route's stops under the ground, lets a body go at the chute's top and holds when it stands where the rock ends: it fell 1.01 units and stands on the drain's spruce deck 5.59 edges down where the rock ends at 5.56, with 21 stops under the ground. So run later, the search's view of the rock agrees with the generated world, which bears out the cause found above. The planet's digest lines did not move (the structure lays stops and paints nothing), `check-repaint` is clean, 172 tests pass and the `live/` sessions take the time they took. Not built: walking the whole route. The tube stops are not the trouble (a passage is air only above the plane through its axis, `floorHeight` in `tubeSpace`, so its floor is at the axis and the stops lie on it); a walk from the sinkhole's bottom chamber fell out of that chamber 1.1 units below the first tube stop onto a lattice girder (concrete), so that chamber opens below into the void (by a breach, mouth or drain of its own), and a walkable route has to keep away from chambers that open downward. The search does now (a sinkhole whose bottom chamber is over the void is refused, and the tree is not searched through a chamber over the void unless it is the drain's), and the drop law still holds on the route it chooses (5.65 edges down where the rock ends at 5.63). A walk of the whole route then stood on cave grass 1.11 units below its first tube stop, so the tube stops' height against the floors the walker reaches is still wrong somewhere. The likely place, read in `tubeTo` and not shown: a link steeper than `STEEP_RISE` is carved as a shaft (`Form.shaft`), a vertical tube with spiral treads and no floor at its axis, so a body set down at a vertex above one falls down it, and the walker has to be led round its treads the way the caves world's own `route` leads a body down the sinkhole's shaft (a stop on each tread, `TREAD_MIDDLE` at `wedgeAngle(tread)` in the shaft's helix); the forced route lays three axis stops on every link whatever its form. Stops per link form are the next step. Done since (`linkStops` in `caves.d`: a passage gets its three axis stops, a shaft a stop on each tread round its helix, and the route starts at its first passage stop rather than at the sinkhole's bottom vertex, which has other links out of it that a body set down there can fall down). With the body set down just over the floor a walk now passes five stops and sticks before the sixth, standing on cave grass 0.04 units across from it with the stop 0.35 units below the floor: a passage's floor is the plane through its axis turned between the two ends' up directions (`floorHeight`), so along a descending link the axis can run under the floor and an axis stop part way down lies in the rock. That guess was wrong (the floor plane contains the axis by construction), and printing the route settled it. The route near spawn is six links: passage, passage, shaft (rising 48.8 degrees at its top, 74.9 at its foot, 1.81 to 2.70 edges deep), passage, passage, shaft, then the chute. The walk passes both passages and sticks at the first shaft: its top treads lie under the rock of the chamber it leaves, and a body on the incoming ramp stands right over them with rock between. Three ways in were tried and undone. (1) Letting shaft space stand against the ramp cut in `spaceAt` holed some ramp floor, so the walker fell through at the second passage onto limestone. That points to another shaft at the same vertex, not on the route, whose space the cut was also hiding. (2) One stop at the shaft's foot, to drop down its core: the core is tilted against gravity, and the body fell past the foot chamber to 4.2 edges. (3) Starting the tread stops at the tread on the shaft's downhill side (tread 2): it is under the same rock, with a dolomite wall at the ramp's end. A search limited to passage links found no route within 300 vertices. So a tilted shaft (one end steeper than `STEEP_RISE`, the other only just past `WALKABLE_RISE`) is not enterable from the chamber at its top. That is a fault in the caves themselves, not in the route: a player meets the same wall. The fix belongs in how a shaft opens into its top chamber, and it needs a render of that chamber before anything else is changed. A view placed by the route's sixth stop rendered concrete under open sky in the windowed program, so there the blueprint's route list does not begin with this route (the placed structure may not have run yet when the view reads it, or other stops come first). A view of the chamber has to find the route's stops by depth, as the law does, not by index. Built so (`routeshaft` in `views.d`: the fifth stop under the ground, looking toward the sixth). It renders inside the cave, and looking down the second passage toward the shaft's chamber shows shale walls closing into a slot, so the passage pinches shut before the chamber. The passage at its top end rises 0 degrees and at its foot 48.8 (the vertices' up directions differ that much across one edge), so the half carved by the lower chamber is a steep ramp and the half carved by the upper one a level tube, and the two halves do not meet. That reading was wrong. Asked directly, both ends' scenes put every passage's floor on its axis at every ninth of the edge, so the halves meet and the render was looking into the curved wall of a 49 degree ramp. Requiring the search's vertices to be open changed nothing and was undone. The real block is the shaft's mouth. A probe of the column over each of the shaft's top treads, as the top chamber's scene sees it, read tread, then seven cells of rock, then air for tread 0, and less rock for each tread after. A shaft's space begins at the plane through its vertex square to its axis, so on a tilted shaft the low side of that plane lies under the chamber's floor and under the incoming ramp, and the ramp cut (`isUnderRamp`) keeps it rock. Letting shaft space stand against the ramp cut opened the mouth. With the route ending the passage before a shaft at its first stop, the walk then took the shaft's first two treads and stalled on the third, 0.026 units off. But the caves world's own `structure/caves` walk broke with it (stuck at waypoint 29, the floor its ring stops expect now opened), so it was undone. A one cell tolerance for this walk only got it one tread further: a tilted shaft's voxel steps drift from its tread geometry a little more on each tread. Walking a tilted shaft tread by tread is the wrong design. The choice belongs to the caves' design: keep shafts upright (form a shaft only when both ends are steep, or tilt its helix with gravity), or give a tilted shaft's mouth a proper opening that both routes are laid to. The law stays the drop down the chute, which holds. Tried on 2026-11-19 as the choice that leaves the world alone: a route through passages and upright shafts only (both ends steeper than `STEEP_RISE`), with the rim stop before every shaft. None exists within 300 vertices of spawn, nor within 1000, so under the planet every way down to a drain passes a tilted shaft. Undone. The walk now waits on the caves' design: a tilted shaft has to become enterable for players first, and its route follows from that. Also tried: opening the mouth only of a shaft that descends from its chamber (`Top.near`), not one that rises into it. The caves world's own walk broke at the same waypoint 29: its sinkhole's bottom chamber has a descending shaft beside the route, and that shaft's opened mouth holes the floor the ring stops cross. Undone. Any opened mouth changes floors that routes already walk wherever a chamber has more than one way down, so the routes have to be laid around the mouths. That is a redesign of the caves' routes, not a local fix. The other half of the choice was tried as well: upright shafts only (`tubeTo` forms a shaft only when both ends are steeper than `STEEP_RISE`). Every cave law held (`caves`, `sinkhole`, `cavenetwork`, `gallery`, `waydown`), but the walk failed worse. The link that is 75 degrees at its foot became a passage, a near-sheer ramp, and the body fell down it and on, ending 2.4 units below the sixth stop on andesite 4.5 edges down. Undone. Neither half alone makes a tilted link walkable: a passage cannot be that steep and a tilted shaft's top is buried. What remains is a tread for tilted shafts that follows gravity rather than the shaft's axis (a stair helix around the vertical through the link, not around the link), and that is a new kind of tube. Tried: a shaft's treads measured along gravity, the ends' ups blended by how far along the shaft a point is, as `floorHeight` blends them, so every tread is level and the two halves agree. The route's tread stops were solved from the same drop. Every cave law held, but the walk stuck at the first shaft stop 0.41 units under the ramp floor again. The shaft's space still begins at the plane through its top vertex square to its axis, and that plane still cuts the low side's upper treads into rock. Undone. A tilted shaft also needs its space to begin at the chamber's floor, which reopens the ramp-cut conflict that broke the caves world's walk. So the remaining step is the shaft's top, not its treads. Tried both together, for tilted shafts only (a `Lean` on the tube, tilted when either end is under `STEEP_RISE`): level treads, mouths that stand against the ramp cut, and route stops solved from the treads' drop. Both walks failed. The caves world's broke at the same waypoint 29, because the shaft beside its sinkhole's route is itself tilted. The forced route fell through the ramp at its fourth stop, the hole the first attempt made. Undone; digests as before. An opened mouth holds a hole wherever another route crosses above it, so any mouth change has to come with routes that keep off the shaft mouths of chambers they cross. That is a change to how both routes are searched, and it is where this stands. One more search filter tried: accept a shaft only when it is steeper than `STEEP_RISE` at its upper end, so that it drops cleanly from its chamber's floor. No route within 300 vertices of spawn, nor within 1000. Under the planet, every way down to a drain passes a shaft tilted at its top. Undone. Then all four together, for tilted shafts only: opened mouths, level treads, the passage before a tilted shaft ending at its first stop (so the walker goes down the ramp into the mouth), and tread stops placed from the level drop. That got furthest. The forced walk went down the ramp, dropped into the shaft's mouth, and stalled only at the first tread, standing on the shaft's limestone 0.05 units below that tread's stop and 0.06 across, just outside the walker's 0.02 tolerance. The caves world's walk still broke at waypoint 29, since the tilted shaft beside its sinkhole route opens under that route's ring stops. Undone; digests as before. The remaining work is two pieces: tread stops placed on the treads' actual top (blending the up directions as the treads do, where the stops used the top end's up alone), and the caves world's sinkhole route moved to keep off the opened mouth of any tilted shaft in its bottom chamber. Both tried, and undone. Tread stops found by bisection on the treads' own drop, and sinkholes with a tilted branch out of their bottom chamber no longer counting as entries (`entryUnder`). The caves world's walk broke at the same waypoint 29 as before, so its sinkhole was not excluded. The opened mouth under its ring stops belongs to a shaft that is not one of the bottom chamber's tree branches, which the entry check cannot see. The forced walk did worse than the last try, ending 0.47 units below its fourth stop. So the hole under the caves world's ring has to be found first, by probing which tube opens there, before the route can be kept off it. One guess tested: `entryUnder`'s branch loop skips the link straight down (and its opposite), so a tilted shaft continuing below the sinkhole's bottom was never checked. Excluding sinkholes with a tilted shaft on any descending link changed the failure, not the outcome: waypoint 29, 0.26 units below, on limestone, which is the failure of the very first all-shafts attempt. The culprit may be the sinkhole's own entrance shaft if that counts as tilted, but that was not shown. Undone; digests as before. The probe still comes first. Probed (printed from the caves world's `route` with tilted mouths opened): the entrance shaft is upright (90 degrees at both ends), and so is every shaft at the sinkhole's bottom. Its four passages leave level and arrive at 45 degrees. So the hole is not in that chamber. Waypoint 29 is on the first branch passage past the ring, and the hole is in that passage's floor near its far end. There the next chamber has a tilted shaft going down, and its opened mouth cuts through the floor of the ramp arriving above it. In general, opening a tilted shaft's mouth turns every ramp that arrives over it into a trapdoor: the same opening the forced walk used as its way in, met from a route that means to walk past. So opened mouths cannot be added without moving routes that only pass by, and an unopened tilted shaft has no reachable top. What remains is a choice about tilted shafts themselves: make the treads begin below the ramps' floors, so the shaft is entered from inside the chamber's own air (its top tread at the dome floor's height under the vertex, not at the plane square to the axis), or stop forming tilted shafts at all. A third, narrower option was weighed: open tilted mouths only on the planet's forced route, so no other cave changes. It cannot be done from `forcedRoute`, because tiles paint over placed structures and the cave tile would close the opening again. It needs the route search moved from `forcedRoute` into the planet's `world` (it only probes, so it can run there), the opened shafts' upper vertices handed to the tiles in `caves.Root` beside `forced`, and `tubeTo`/`spaceAt` opening a tilted shaft's mouth only when its upper vertex is listed. That still leaves the first-tread stall seen when mouths were opened (0.05 below and 0.06 across its stop), so it is three changes with one known failure behind them. Then built and undone: it cannot work under the current plugin API. With the route search moved into the planet's `world` (to fill `caves.Root.opened` before `sink` hands `Root` to the tiles), the search found no route at all and laid 0 stops, since the world's own structure probes before the graph around it exists. That is why `forcedRoute` is a placed structure. A placed structure has no way to hand data to tiles, though, because the tiles' data is copied when `tileEdges` is called. The caves world's walk passed throughout, so the narrow opening itself left other caves alone. Finishing this needs a plugin ABI change: a call by which a placed structure adds hashes that a tile can ask about, as `probePort` asks about ports. Digests as before after the undo. That call alone would break determinism. Placed structures run as the graph grows toward their site, but tiles run in no fixed order on workers, so a tile that asked before `forcedRoute` ran would carve the shaft shut and the same place would generate by visiting order. A correct version needs an engine guarantee that the route is laid before any tile can ask: either the query runs a pending placed structure first (from a worker, which today only the main thread may do), or the route moves to a pass that always runs before the tiles near it. That is an engine change to structure and tile scheduling, not a plugin call.

Found on 2026-11-19 in the quality loop: `caapi --screenshot <png> flight` crashes (exit 132, a failed assert in release). It is `assert(atomicLoad(job) == 0)` in `WorkerPool.open` (`base/parallel.d`), reached from `Renderer.meshChanged` <- `updateChunks` <- `renderFrame` <- `SettledFrame.render` <- `renderSettled`. So a parallel pass is still open when the mesher starts its own. The `flight` view is the only screenshot view that calls `view.live()`, and in live play the renderer starts the tile planning ahead after its chunk update (`LocalWorld.planAhead`). CLAUDE.md says `SettledFrame.render` must call `joinAhead` last; the likely fault is that a second `renderFrame` inside one settled render (or the first one after a live step) meets the open ahead pass before its mesh update. Not yet read: `SettledFrame` in `app/main.d`. The other 60-odd views rendered this round did not crash, and none of them calls `live()`. Fixed the same day: `SettledFrame.render` rendered once, then rendered again while `meshBacklog() > 0` without joining the plan-ahead pass the first render left open, and joined it only after the loop. It now calls `game.world.joinAhead()` before each further render, as the play loop does after its wait for the display. The `flight` view renders (14 frames) and was pitched down 60 degrees, since it had looked level into the sky; its script passes headless.

Views still framed badly, from the same loop: `cavesglow` stands at the first cave-grass cell `firstOf` finds among the loaded chunks, which lies at a chamber's rim, so every pitch or shift tried (pitch -25, -5, -10, rise 0.12 or 0.35) ends looking into the wall's corner. It needs a search for a floor cell with open air in front of it, not a different angle. Tried and undone: standing at each of the first 64 cave-grass cells in the loaded chunks and keeping the first where `Script.targetMaterial` finds nothing ahead. None passed, which open chambers make implausible, so the test was wrong. Either `look` turns relative to the facing a placement leaves, or the target ray meets the floor cell the camera stands over. Read since: `Script.look` adds to the facing (`game.look(yaw, pitch)`, which `glide` and `ease` call with small steps that sum), so the search's `look(0, 0)` changed nothing. Every probe aimed wherever the placement left the camera, quite possibly down at the floor. A search that works has to put the facing in a known state before each probe, which `Script` gives no call for yet. Tried again with a levelled facing (look 180 degrees up, so pitch stops at straight up, then 90 down): still none of 64. So the facing was not the whole cause. Aiming reaches farther than these chambers are wide, and a level ray meets a far wall from anywhere in them. "Nothing within reach ahead" is the wrong test. A test of open room has to measure the distance to the hit and want it above a few cells, and `Script` does not give that distance. Undone; the view keeps its old framing. Done since: `LocalWorld.isClearAhead(distance)` casts the camera's centre ray as `targetMaterial` does, with a reach of the caller's choosing, and `Script.isClearAhead(Length)` passes it on. The glow view (`cavesGlow` with `openFloorOf` in `views.d`) levels the facing, then stands at each cave-grass cell in turn, up to 256, and keeps the first with 0.4 units clear ahead. It looks up into a chamber's dome scattered with glowing cells. All seven caves view scripts pass, and the app, headless and web builds succeed. `mazelights` shows the walkways' undersides with bright flecks beyond. Whether those are lamps the view is named for or the void's stars was not checked against the lattice's maze code. `lattice.content` gives the lattice's `lantern` and its `star` the same material (`base:light-panel`), so the two cannot be told apart in a render. The view shows lit panels against the dark either way. Telling which they are means reading where `lattice.d` places the maze's lanterns. Read since: the maze has no lanterns. Its lights are its own stars, which `mazeStar` in the maze tile paints as `materials.star` cells in rhythmic columns from `-MAZE_STAR_FARTHEST` to `-MAZE_STAR_NEAREST` below each quarter. `mazelights` looks up at the walkways' undersides from among them, so the flecks it shows are the maze's lights, and the view shows its subject. Closed. `risings` and `risingsup` (3 and 4 units out at a right angle from spawn, looking 30 degrees up) show towers and a tall tree but no risen ground: looking 55 degrees up from there shows empty sky, so no ground rises over that spot any more. They need to be pointed at a rising found by search, as `terracesrising` finds the terraces with `ensureRisingFrom`. `zenith` (3 units out, looking straight up) shows only sky, likely for the same reason. Done: `risings(back, pitch)` finds the terraces' foot as `terracesrising` does, backs off and looks up. `risings` (2 back, 25 up) shows the risen ground in the sky with its connector, `risingsup` (1.2 back, 45 up) the connector climbing toward it, and `zenith` (0.3 back, 85 up) looks straight up the connector's well. `island` drops 6.6 units under spawn in walking mode, lands, backs 0.6 and rises 0.35, then looks 30 degrees up. That shows girders, rocks and a wooden platform overhead, not an island. Looking 30 degrees down shows a pale column among fragments, with no island either, so the body most likely lands on lattice stone or a girder rather than an island's turf. Checked with `walk.lookBelow`: it lands on `base:marble`, which the lattice's content uses for a colossus `limb` and one of its platform `floors`. So the view stands on void furniture, not on an island. The view needs to find an island (the lattice's `island` andesite with `turf` grass, `lattice.content`) and stand off it, not another pitch. Done: `islandFound` turns streaming on, drops 6.6 units, waits for loading and stands 0.3 over and 0.6 back from the first grass cell loaded, looking 30 degrees down. It shows a floating island with grass, soil and a tree among the drifting fragments. The old `islandBelow` is deleted. `libraryvoid` (3 units below the underside, 5 edges from the library's foot) shows asteroids among stars but none of the floating bookshelves the library's void holds above its asteroids (`bookVoid` in `lattice.d`: shelves above a shelf layer, asteroids below). It most likely sits below the shelf layer; not checked. Fixed: dropping 0.5 units in place of 3 (`LIBRARY_DROP`) puts it among the shelves. Spruce shelves lined with coloured books meet at a joint in the void, with asteroids drifting past. At 1.5 it still showed only asteroids. `cityedge` (4 units out from the city's start) sat inside a building at 0.15 up. At 1.0 up, looking 20 degrees down, it stands outside between two towers. It shows no edge, and whether the city world has one (or its blocks run on without end) was not checked, so the view's name may describe something that is not there. Checked since: the city world's root is one city ground that `blocks.tile` paints with blocks on every face (`plugins/city/plugin.d`), so the city has no edge. The view is renamed `cityfar`, for what it shows: the city 4 units out from its start. Closed. `under90` (9 units under spawn) has a pale surface filling half the frame, one turn or the other, with the maroon deep void and fragments in the rest. The camera seems to sit against a colossus's marble at that depth, so a turn does not clear it; a small step back or down would. Done: `underSpawn` takes a step back after it turns (0 for the rest of the series), and `under90` steps 0.6 back, so the colossus's body and limbs read as a shape in the deep void. `wildwell` (1.98 down the well under a port, backed 0.3) sits against the well's slate wall, with spruce treads at arm's length. Looking 50 degrees down instead of 10 shows the same slate and a tread, with dim void below where the well opens under the crust. The camera is too close to the wall for a turn to help: it needs placing at the well's middle (no back, `terrainPort`'s second number 0), not backing into the wall. Tried: with no step back it faces a slate corner, likely the stair's central post, which is worse. Kept at 0.3. Showing the stair needs a place on the treads' ring, between post and wall, with the facing along the stair; not done. Improved since: backing 0.15 as `wildwelljoint` does, looking 15 degrees down, puts spruce treads in the lower right with the slate walls around them. The stair is still only partly in view. `vaultspassage` (the nearest red carpet, turned to yaw 270, backed 0.05) stands against a sandstone wall corner with sky above it, and yaw 90 faces another wall. The carpet cell it finds is likely on a terrace, not in a passage, so it needs a carpet cell with open room ahead (`openFloorOf` with streaming on, as `cavesglow` and `under25` do), not another turn. Tried and undone: that search found a terrace carpet cell with open air ahead, the same kind of spot. A passage also has a ceiling, so the search needs a second probe straight up (a ceiling within a few cells) to tell a passage from a terrace. Tried and undone too: with a probe straight up for a ceiling within 0.3 units, the same frame came back. The first terrace cell passed both, likely because something within 0.3 overhead (the wall's crest) counts as a ceiling; not confirmed. Finding a real passage needs the probe up to want the ceiling's whole span covered, or a search from the vaults' route stops, which run through their halls and passages. Tried the route: standing at its tenth stop looking toward the twelfth shows the same kind of place, red sandstone walls with sky above. So the vaults' passages are open to the sky, as their halls' roofs are, and the view was standing in a passage all along. Closed as no fault. The view keeps its framing, which sits close to one wall. `vaultsgallery` (from the route's end) is tilted and cramped against a wall, though the vaults' roofs show. The world menu lists both "terrain" and "planet", and they are one world: `world-digest` gives identical lines for the default terrain and for `--worldgen structure planet`. Each keeps its own save (`saves/<world>-<mode>...`), so a player can play the same world under two names. Closed as by design: menu entry 0 is the `terrain` worldgen (`pickOf` in `plugins/game/restart.d`), whose world is whatever the content's `terrain` node names, and the base content names the planet. A mod that sets `world` on that node changes entry 0 and leaves the planet entry alone, so the two agree only while the content picks the planet. `wildwell` reframed: it now looks up the well from 1.9 units down, at the wooden treads spiraling up to the daylight at the port, where it used to face a slate corner. `vaultsgallery` keeps its yaw (looking back level along the route puts a sandstone wall across most of the frame) and is pitched up from 20 to 35 degrees, so the cursor no longer rests on the near wall and its block tooltip no longer covers the picture. Open observation: `mazeabove` shows the target tooltip "spruce wood" while nothing under the crosshair is outlined and the crosshair sits over the starfield. The tooltip is `game.aim` (`buildTarget` in `plugins/game/hud.d`), so the aim ray hits a spruce block that the frame does not show at its center. Not a stale aim: `Game.step` runs `updateAim` after `updateView`, on the frame's final camera. What is left is a real hit along the center ray, most likely a dark spruce post down through the maze's gap, too small and dark against the black to show its outline. Not confirmed by casting; closed as not a bug unless a later view shows a tooltip with plainly nothing in reach. `stars` shows the same thing in the deep's fog ("marble" over dark open space). The aim reaches `ClientConfig.reach`, 0.5 units by default (`plugins/game/config.d`), and under a thick fog a block that close can already be blended into the fog color, so the player is told about a block they cannot see and may mine it. A design note, not a fix: capping the aim at the distance where the current fog leaves a block visible would make the target always one that is drawn. Tried and undone: `castCenterRay` capped at `fogDistance(viewRadius(), FOG_LOAD_THRESHOLD)` passed every test but left the `stars` tooltip as it was, so the fog there reaches past the 0.5 aim and the hit is not hidden by fog. The "fogged block" explanation is wrong for `stars`; what the ray hits there, and why it does not show, is not traced. Probed with `isClearAhead` from the view script (5.5 and 5.7 edges under the underside, in the app and headless): the centre ray is blocked within 0.05, so the hit is at the eye. Either the camera stands in a marble cell (the near plane hiding it, as in the buried views), or the ray stops at a chunk not generated yet, which the scripts only make on demand. Telling the two apart needs `GraphCastHit`'s cell checked against the chunk's state. Done: the hit chunk is `populated` and the cell is marble, so the camera stood inside one of the deep's marble masses. At `STARS_EDGES` 6.0 (from 5.5) the centre ray meets nothing within reach and the tooltip is gone. So the aim-reach note above has no case behind it: both stray tooltips seen (`mazeabove`'s spruce, `stars`' marble) were real hits, and `stars`' was a buried camera. A sweep of every view for the same fault (a temporary check in `Script.run` that the centre ray meets nothing within 0.01 of the eye) failed only `mazeabove`: it stood on the maze foot's axis (`ColumnSpot(0, 0, 0)`) inside a spruce post, and 0.6 up it was still inside. At `ColumnSpot(3, 0, 3)` it is clear and the tooltip is gone. All 206 views now have a clear eye. The sweep is now permanent in the headless runner: `views` and `films` run each script with `Eye.clear` (`runTest` in `app/headless.d`), which fails a script whose camera ends with the centre ray blocked within `EYE_ROOM` (0.01) of the eye, so a buried camera fails `views` instead of waiting to be noticed in a render. `tests` and `claims` run `Eye.unchecked`, since a play session may end against a wall. Related, seen in `backroomsworks`: thin black lines run the full height of the right third of the frame and slant across its top. They look like the target outline of a cell right against the camera (the view stands 1.275 units down the backrooms well, beside its wall), drawn in perspective so close that its edges cross the screen. Traced: pitched 10 degrees up, the lines move with the scene, so they are geometry, and the tooltip still names concrete while the crosshair lies over a far wall. The camera stands inside a concrete cell whose faces the near plane cuts away, so the aim hits the cell around the eye and its outline's edges sweep across the frame. Not a renderer seam. The view needs a camera spot clear of the well's wall; `overPort` takes no sideways offset, so the fix is a view helper that steps off the port before looking. Tried stepping 0.1 back before looking: that put the camera against another concrete wall, worse. Undone. The right fix is to find open floor the way `openFloorOf` does for the caves views, not to guess offsets. Tried that too: a search of 16 spots (four headings, up to 0.15 back from the port, 1.275 down) for one whose center ray runs 0.1 or more before hitting anything found none, so that whole stretch of the well is inside solid cells and the view only shows the works because the near plane cuts away the cell the camera is in. Undone. The view needs a different anchor (a material of the works, through `walk.nearestAround` as `inStack` does) rather than a spot near the port. Done: `backroomsworks` is now `inStack` on grey brick (the boiler biome's wall in `rooms:works`) 1.5 edges down the well. It stands in a works corridor with an ordinary block outline, and the lines are gone. The same lines were in `backroomswellbelow` and `vaultswellbelow`, which put the camera on the port's axis with `overPort`, inside the spiral well's newel (`stairDown` paints `Span(-1, 0)` there). Both now use `downWell`, 0.15 off the axis, pitched 45 degrees down the treads around the newel. `overPort` is left for `vaultsport`, which stands above ground. `vaultscave` stood 1.85 edges down the vaults well and showed only the well's stair; at 2.85 edges it shows the stair coming down into the cave chamber (red sandstone floor, rough walls), which is what its name promises. The controls screen clipped most action names ("walk forw", "step righ", "eat what"): at the 360-row HUD only 9 rows fit, so 29 bindings take 4 columns of about 18 characters, and each row gave the label a fixed half. Each row now splits by need (`labelShare` in `plugins/game/screens.d`): a key gets its own width and the label the rest, and only when both cannot fit do they share in proportion to their lengths. Labels with one-character keys now show whole; rows whose key is long ("left mouse", "wheel down") still clip both. `CONTROL_LABEL_COLUMNS` is gone with the fixed half. The lab drew every reaction as a live button even with "0/1" of its reagent, while the dose routes beside them already go dim without a dose; `synthesisButton` now disables a reaction whose reagent is not held, so only what can run is raised. The chemistry sheet's dose routes did the same for their devices ("inhale (needs an inhaler)" was live with no inhaler); a route now needs its `DoseRoute.device` carried as well as a dose. Notes were cut at half the screen ("planted a flower with no genes of its own;" lost its second half); `buildNotes` now wraps a note onto up to `MOST_NOTE_LINES` rows. The line breaking (`lineEnd`, `nextLineStart` in `hud.d`) is the journal's, which `wrappedRows` in `screens.d` now shares instead of its own copy. The nearest beacon's distance over the compass was the one HUD label without a panel, cyan text that vanished over cyan blocks (`blocks`); it now sits on a `PANEL` rect like the others. Open: `bigtree` (1 back and 0.5 up from spawn, pitched 30 down) shows a field of orange trees with only a small dark crown far off, not the big tree by spawn that `overview` shows. Level, and turned round looking up, it showed only sky. Looking 30 up showed only sky too. The spawn is the big tree's own entrance (`tree.entrance(SPAWN...)` in `plugins/planet/plugin.d`, the `tree` kind risen `TREE_RISE` at `TREE_EDGES` right of the root), yet a camera 1 back and 0.5 up sees it in none of four directions. So either the tree stands behind the spawn's facing at some other bearing, or it is the small dark crown in frame; placing the view from the tree's own site (as `risings` does for terraces with `ensureRisingFrom`) would settle it. Its numbers are named constants above the view. `zenith` (`risings`, 0.3 back from the terraces' foot, looking up) stands in the tower's glass wall (outline sweep, tooltip glass) yet gives the clearest frame, straight up the shaft; 0.1 and 0.2 back both landed in the marble at the axis. Kept at 0.3. A clean version stands on a floor inside the shaft. The tower is not the terraces' own: they are a bare risen ground (`plugins/skies/plugin.d`), and what rises to them is the connector (`footTile`), so the floors to stand on are the connector's, from `plugins/connector`. Backed farther: at 0.45 the camera is outside the tower facing its wall, at 1.0 it sees sky with the risen ground in a corner. 0.3 gives the best frame and passes the `views` eye check (nothing within `EYE_ROOM`), so it stays. The four `around` views rose half a unit over spawn and pitched 8 up, so with the ground curving away they were mostly sky (`around180` kept only a strip of treetops); pitched 10 down they keep the trees in the lower third and `around0` the big tree's whole trunk. `cavesglow` found its cave grass and then looked 15 up at the dome; looking 15 down it keeps the glowing dome and shows the grass it was placed on. `caveschandelier` and `cavescrystal` backed `CAVES_BACK` (0.45) off their find into granite and basalt (outline sweep, tooltip naming the rock); `cavesFound` takes the back distance as a parameter now, defaulting to `CAVES_BACK` so `cavesgypsum` is as it was, and both back 0.2: the chandelier hangs overhead in the open chamber and the crystal view stands among its glass beams. `backroomstop` rose 0.55 over the route's end into a white-brick wall (outline sweep); at 0.7 it is out of the wall but still faces a building's side close up, and turned to 300 it sees only the backrooms' fog. Kept at 0.7 and 120, no longer buried but still a weak frame. Open: `libraryconnector` (`backFromRouteEnd`, 0.8 back from the connector route's end in `risenlibrary`) stands in the library's hall facing a carpeted block, and turned round it sees more of the hall; the connector is in neither. The route ends inside the library, so the connector is below that end, not behind it: a view of it would back off the route's last stop under the library floor, or stand at the connector's foot as the `*foot` views do. Done the second way: `libraryconnector` is now `connectorFoot`, 0.3 up and 0.8 back from the route's start, and the connector tower fills the frame with the risen library on top. `libraryconnectorup` backed off the route's end too and framed one carpet-and-spruce block close up; it is now `connectorFromAside` like the other connector views, looking up the tower to the library on top. `under25` lands inside rock 2.5 units under spawn. Pointing it at the first cave-grass cell with room ahead (`openFloorOf`, as `cavesglow` now does) found none among 256 on the planet, with 0.4 or 0.2 units of room. Whether the planet holds little loaded cave grass there or the probe faces walls was not worked out. Letting the probe turn a quarter at a time at each cell, so it could find a passage's long axis, still found none, so facing walls is not the cause. Most likely little or no cave grass is loaded near that point, but that was not counted. Counted since: zero cells, because a script makes chunks on demand only until it calls `live()`, and the search never did; `cavesglow` calls `view.live()` and waits for loading. With the same two lines, `under25` (`underCave` in `views.d`) dives 2.5 units under spawn and stands in a planet cave: cave-grass ledges, glowing ceiling cells and a passage opening ahead. Closed. Earlier: no cave floor lies within reach of that point, so a cave view there needs a different depth or target. The law stays the drop down the chute, which holds. The law checks the drop, and the rest is laid for the walker to come. An open question found on the way, measured and not explained: at the wild port nearest spawn (a square well of spruce, the rock's underside read 2.005 edges down from the script's side, so by `wellDepthOf` a well of 3 edges ending on a deck), the 12 by 12 square of the deck read, in the frame `checkFlight` uses, all air at the bottom of edge 1, 4 spruce and 80 other solid cells at the bottom of edge 2, and 144 solid cells, none of them spruce, at the bottom of edge 3. The render `wildwellbottom` shows the last treads on grey brick. So either the deck is not painted where `landing` means it, or the plugin's crust at the mouth differs from what the script reads, or that layer is something else; it needs the square well's path (`stairDown` with `WellShape.square`) read against those cells before a law is written. Read since: `stairDown` cuts the port's 8 by 8 core open over the whole edge before laying treads, so a layer whose core is solid, as at the bottom of edge 3, is one where either no well edge was painted or something filled the column after it. The material there was read as grey brick, which is not the colossus (gabbro) but one of the lattice's platform floors (`floors` in `lattice:materials`). So the layer is a lattice platform: the well's last edge has its lower end in the open, the lattice hangs that edge, and `platform` laid a floor at both of its ends, the lower one over the well's deck and the upper one painted into the rock, across part of the well's shaft. Fixed (2026-11-18): `platform` fills (only cells the ground generated as air) where it painted. In the open nothing changes (the render `wildwellbottom` is as it was: the treads come down onto the grey brick platform, which is the floor under the stair); against rock a platform now stops where the rock is, so it can no longer plug a shaft or show in a cave floor, which is also the "lattice in deep cave floors" weak spot in section 1 for platforms. The same day the beams followed: a strut, its double and its two joints fill as well, so a pole that straddles the underside stops at the rock and no longer runs up into it as far as the vertex above (that weak spot is fixed, the pole stairs excepted, which stay painted because the plan's way back has them go on up through the rock). The planet's digest lines changed in their materials again and not in their occupancy (`digest ... materials b20bbd211bac467601252b044e422ae3`, `deep ... materials a2608d25cf0d586f2fb7000108b7d685`, occupancy as before), the lattice world's lines did not move (it has no rock), and it is cheaper: within 2.5 units of spawn 678 chunks are uniformly solid where 659 were and 2742 are dense where 2761 were, `bench-gen` parameters 1577 to 1611 ms. Structure, falls, live and hidden tests, repaint on the planet and forget on lattice clean. The planet's digest lines changed in their materials and not in their occupancy (`digest ... materials d0eb254289992e206b0e574132234b01 occupancy 4795fce6dea3bc3499dc73afb53422a9`, `deep ... materials 56cd8bbdf003e68c70a7e673adc84ebc occupancy 6b70f72354e30317fee4b0382c11cb5a`): the same cells are solid, and platform cells that had replaced rock are rock again. Lattice: `digest ... materials 845a06471b91300489d56bb7ce761b7a occupancy 9707baa8419c788e74170e0c34005d86`, `deep ... materials ba62d2a4247ceed5513efc7785e90931 occupancy 3a356b31df9ca15db07a2980b27e49df` (no earlier lines from the same day to compare). 169 tests, `check-repaint` on planet and lattice and `check-forget` on lattice clean; `bench-gen` 1598 to 1654 ms with the same 341333 brushes. Not built: mouths, the forced route and its laws. Still open, and the census is the tool for it: under the default world the caves near spawn are a few trees, not a network. Measured out to 4 units: 44 spaces, the largest 20 percent of the air; with every cross link open (`cross-odds` 1) 46 spaces and 20 percent; with twice the sinkholes (`root-odds` 0.12) 53 spaces holding nearly twice the air and the largest still 20 percent, at no measurable cost to `bench-drive`. So neither knob joins them: the open trees under the default world lie too far apart for a passage between neighbouring chambers to reach. The census was then given a second band, 1.9 to 5.2 edges, below the depth (`HALL_DEPTH`, 1.95) where a kept chamber can be open by itself (a hall, `hall-odds` 0.3) and not only by its tree reaching a sinkhole; both bands leave out cells below the rock's underside, so the void between the masses is not counted. Near spawn (2.5 units) the deep band holds 267699 cells of cave air in 49 spaces, the largest 16 percent; out to 4 units 5.7 million cells in 570 spaces, the largest 8 percent (the upper band, with the underside left out: 7 spaces and 59 percent near spawn, 28 and 26 percent out to 4). So there is far more open cave below than above, and it is in pieces too. Twice the halls (`hall-odds` 0.6) gave 764 spaces and the largest 13 percent out to 4 units, with 40 percent more cave air: no jump to one network, so the pieces are not kept apart by how rare open chambers are. Taken to be the rock masses and the void windows between them, which a passage between two neighbouring vertices cannot cross; not shown. Set back to 0.3. The next thing to try is a link that knows about the underside: passages that follow the rock around a window, or a level of galleries kept inside the thin crust everywhere. Joining them needs a longer link (a gallery that runs on through rock between two trees, or a level of caves that every tree reaches down to), which is a design choice, not a tuning; both settings are as they were |
| C. The fall and the way back | the builder was stopped before it reported. Its work is in the tree and its tests pass (`falls/`, `save/round-trip`), the bottom under a ground included (`api.abyss`); nobody has reviewed it or looked at it in play |
| D. Ports that belong to their ground | the engine step and two of the three rules are built and checked. `api.portRule(ground, most, rule, data, size)` (plugin ABI 33) gives a ground a rule of its own: the engine rolls each vertex as it always did, against `most`, the largest share of vertices the rule can take, and asks the rule only about the vertices under it, handing it the asking host's `Api`, the vertex's site and the roll, so a vertex that is not a candidate costs what it cost before. The engine keeps its three refusals (the world's start, a risen ground's foot, a vertex where a ground rises) and asks the rule of the ground the asker is over, by the ground's tag (`KindTag.init` for a root with no kind); a ground without a rule keeps the flat roll of `api.ports`; a rising kind carries its rule with it (`RisingKind.mostPorts`, `ports`). Scripts ask through the running world's host (`probeWorldPort`, used by `walk.ensurePort`). A rule is handed a site that lies in the ground whichever way the asker stands (`groundedSite` in `structures.d`: an axis of a site is named by the cell face it crosses, the ground through a vertex is the plane of one of the three faces of the site's node at that corner, and one pitch or roll brings the y axis to cross it), so the four ground faces around the vertex are the site's own and those of its three left turns. A rule that calls `pick` or `random` is refused, and the world stops with the message. Laws, on three rigs the caves plugin registers: `structure/portrule` (`everyport`), `structure/portfaces` (`facedport`: 81 vertices asked upright and lying answer the same; with the grounding taken out only 37 did) and `structure/portroll` (`rolledport`). Both digest lines, `structure/well` and `check-repaint` on city and planet are unchanged by all of it. The city's rule (`blocks.portAt`): a vertex takes the district of the face with the lowest hash among its four and is a port when its roll is under that district's `ports` share, which is 0.15 for `blocks:works` and 0 for the others, so about as many ports as the flat roll gave but all of them in works districts; the city world registers it for its root and the shared kinds register it for every risen city. The vaults' rule (`portAt` in the vaults plugin): a vertex under `port-share` (0.05) is a port when the hall on its lowest-hash face holds a spire; the vaults world registers it and the vaults' rising kind carries it. With them: `content-check` 0 problems, both digest lines unchanged, `check-repaint` clean on city, vaults and planet, the city tests' wall times the same with the city's rule as with the flat roll (three runs each), and the view `citywell` rendered and looked at (the port lies at a street corner by a parking lot and a brick warehouse). `walk.ensurePort` searches 4096 sites now, since the nearest works-district port can be farther than the nearest flat-rolled one was. No law says a city port lies in a works district: a script cannot see districts. The backrooms' rule (`layout.portAt`): a vertex takes the lowest level's biome on its lowest-hash face and is a port when its roll is under that biome's `well` share, 0.09 for `rooms:sump`, 0.06 for `rooms:cellar` and 0 for the others; the backrooms world registers it for its root and the shared kinds for every risen backrooms. The three rules find the face that owns a vertex with one helper, `ownerAround` in `turtle.d`. The backrooms tests take the same wall time with the rule as without (three runs each). The city's port is dressed (`portFrame` in `blocks.d`): a kerb around the pit and a steel frame over it, six cells up, on every lot that meets the port (`kerb` and `frame` of `blocks:materials`). Law: `structure/cityport` (steel stands within the pit's width of the port's vertex); `citywell` rendered and looked at again. The vaults' port is dressed too (`isRim` and `isRubble` in the vaults plugin): a rim of the hall's inlay one cell high around the hole, broken at two cells in each quarter, and three blocks of the hall's wall stone as rubble beside it; view `vaultsport`, rendered and looked at; it has no law, since the rim is whatever the hall's palette is. A well's stair is made of its ground's own material: the caves content names one per ground tag (`caves-well`: steel under a city, concrete under the backrooms, marble under the vaults) and limestone stays for the rest. Law: `structure/vaultwell` (a body let go in a port's well under the vaults lands on a marble tread). A ground with no kind has no tag to name, so the `city` and `backrooms` worlds' own roots keep limestone while the same grounds risen in the default world get theirs; `structure/well` still lands on limestone for that reason. The backrooms' stairwell and the wells' linings are built since (the 2026-11-14 block "the owner having asked"). Not built: a tag for the standalone worlds' roots |
| E. The hidden stair to the vaults | built and checked (see the 2026-11-14 block "the owner having asked") |
| F. Void worlds: the interface | layers by depth built in the planet, and since 2026-11-18 they are content: `planet:voids` lists `planet-void` layers, each with how far under the crust it reaches (`until`, edges), what fills it (`lattice`, `drift`, `branch`) and whether stars hang in it; the first layer the depth falls in paints, and the colossus is painted under all of them. The two numbers that were settings (`lattice-layer` 3, `fractal-layer` 5) are the first two layers' `until`. The planet's two digest lines are as before, they move when the lattice layer's `until` is set to 4 (checked, and set back), and planet `bench-gen` reads the same. The registry is built (2026-11-18, at the owner's word; plugin ABI 38): a plugin registers a void world with `registry.addVoid(name, edge)`, where `edge` (`VoidEdge` in `api.d`) fills one edge in the open from its two end heights and the ground's bottom, as the lattice's passes always did; a world's edge tile hands an edge to one by the tag of its name with `api.paintVoid`, which answers false for a name nobody registered. The lattice plugin registers its five passes (`lattice:hang`, `lattice:drift`, `lattice:branch`, `lattice:stars`, `lattice:colossus`), and `planet:voids` names them as text: each layer's `world`, an optional `over` painted on top (the stars), and `everywhere`, painted under every layer (the colossus); a layer that names a void world nobody registered stops the world with a message. The planet's code names no void world any more, so a mod can put its own under the default world by registering it and overriding a `planet-void`. The planet's digest lines are as they were, `check-repaint` on the planet and `check-forget` on lattice are clean, `content-check` finds nothing and 169 tests pass; `bench-gen` parameters 1625 to 1644 ms against 1632 to 1671 before (the registry keeps each name's tag, so a lookup hashes nothing). The look per layer (step 6) is built since, the same day, with no engine change: of the under-looks whose start lies above a point the engine already takes the deepest (`underAt` in `horospheres.d`), and an under-crust look starts at its depth below the crust, so each `planet-void` layer names its own `look` and `fog`, set by the planet's `deepen` from the previous layer's `until` down. The lattice layer keeps `lattice:look` at fog 3.5 as `lattice.deepen` gave it; the drift layer is `planet:drift` (a deeper blue, fog 3) and the branch layer `planet:branch` (a dusky red, fog 2.5), both defined in the planet's content. The library keeps `lattice.deepen`. Content binding gained `Text` (a field read as its text, where `Named` keeps only the tag), which is how a look's name reaches the API. The planet's digest lines did not move (looks are not voxels), `content-check` finds nothing, the structure and falls tests pass, and `under90` was rendered and looked at: the branch layer's limbs stand in a red-black fog where the first darker try could not be told from the lattice's. Of F's design, the surface showing through (step 4) is in the tree for the things it named that still exist: wells that go down to where the rock ends and drains come through the crust and end on decks in the void (`landing` in `caves.d`), and `structure/waydown` checks such a deck where the rock ends; grove footings, its other example, went with the groves at the owner's word. Not built of step 4: the general helper in `turtle.d` that climbs from a void vertex to the ground vertex above it, for a void world to tie onto whatever comes through |
| G. Void worlds: the nightmare | G1 to G5 built; G6 built as a ground that hangs (the maze, 2026-11-14) |
| H. Under built ground | built: H1, H2, H3 and H4 (themes and air). Since 2026-11-18 each built ground and risen wild ground has a cave stack of its own, in content (`caves-stack`) |
| I. Backrooms from several tables | built: fields and the subtype table, plan pools, rare rooms, uses and edges (the 2026-11-14 block "the owner having asked") |
| J. The library's rooms | J1's first piece built and checked: in a study, each carrel's desk on each story holds a lab in 1 of 27 and a press in 3 of 27, decided by a hash of the quarter, the carrel and the story (`station-lots`, `lab-lots` and `press-lots` in the library's settings; the blocks are `press` and `lab` of `library:materials`). A reading hall's table holds a bench at its end on 9 of 27 hall floors (`bench-lots`; the block is `bench` of `library:materials`). Laws: `structure/librarypress` (the nearest press to the entrance is opened where it stands and presses a living block under survival rules) and `structure/librarybench` (the nearest bench opens where it stands). J2 is built and checked as well: 3 faces in 100 are staff rooms (`staff-odds`, taken from the galleries' share), each quarter with a row of steel lockers two cells high, a counter with a sink of water in it and a workbench at its end (`locker`, `sink` and `workbench` of `library:materials`). Law: `structure/librarystaff` (the workbench opens where it stands and steel stands within the room's width of it). Views: `librarystudy` and `librarystaff`, rendered and looked at. Both digest lines are unchanged, `check-repaint` is clean on library and planet, and the library's walk, live and flight tests take the same wall time with the stations as without (three runs each). The scripts find a painted block with `walk.ensureNearest`, which searches the cells around a place and then rings of places around it. Since built: J3, rumors read from pamphlets (2026-11-18); J4, the catalog as a desk in the atriums; the staff room's own floor, ceiling tile and wallpaper, and the catalog desk's shape kept in the save (2026-11-18). Doorway marks were the alternative to the desk and are not built. **Assumed**, as the scout's concern: a painted press, lab or pamphlet can be broken and carried off |
| K. Debug scenes in the creative menu | built and checked: under creative rules the world menu lists every debug structure after the worlds, marked `~`; a pick is a `WorldPick` (a world or a scene and its number); a scene is never saved, from the menu or from `--worldgen debug`; the keys for the previous and next world step within the list the player is in. Laws: `ui/scenes`. View: `menuscenes`, rendered and looked at. Not exercised: entering a scene in the window or the browser, which only the front ends do |
| L. Landmarks that are real | the watchtower, the crown, the palace and the trail recur in the default world, painted by its edge tile; the highways, the shelters and the hamlets were built and taken out again at the owner's word ("After the owner played the build of 2026-11-14"). Babel's floors since hold something to find (2026-11-18): each room of its core, one every 16 cells up the column, has a pamphlet on a lectern of the stair's stone in the corner away from its door (`reading` of `babel:materials`), which holds a rumor as the library's do. Babel is the tower of the library of Babel, and the rumors are the library's own. Law: `structure/babelrumor`; view `babelroom`, rendered (the pamphlet in the corner of the sandstone room; without the lectern it all but vanished against the floor). Babel's repaint differs nowhere and the planet's two digest lines did not move; planet `bench-gen` parameters 1628 to 1689 ms against 1653 to 1776 before, three runs each, 12 brushes more. The arbor's decks too, the same day: each deck sets a breeding bench into its rail, half way between two lanterns (`bench` of `arbor:materials`). Law: `structure/arborbench` (the bench nearest a deck stands 3.4 over the ground and opens where it stands); the arbor's climb is still walked through all 97 stops. Arbor `bench-gen` parameters 17.9 to 18.1 ms against 16.8 to 19.2, one brush more; repaint differs nowhere; the planet's digest lines did not move. No view: from beside the trunk the deck is behind leaves. The bench was put there on the reading that ash, and so mutation, rises with height; measured in the law, that is false: the odds at the bench are 0.066 and at the foot of the tree 0.095, because the air follows each node's climate (relief and strata of the land), not how high the body is. So the bench is something to find and use on the deck, not a better place to breed. Making height matter to the air is an idea for `ideas.md` ("Up as an economy"), not built |
| M. Threes in what is held and needed | pieces 1 to 5 built and checked: the recipes as signed (`base.content`), a dose from three extract, a stack of 81, no cap on compounds, and the queue's core. The queue is jobs: a craft request moves what it needs out of the inventory into its job when queued, what its feeding steps make stays in the job, the last step's product and anything left over go to the inventory, and cancelling any step returns the whole job. A lab reaction pays its reagent and dose when queued and is refused without them. The queue has no size; the screen shows a run of the same craft as one slot with a count and wraps. Crafting time now carries over from one craft to the next inside a step. Laws: `flat/queue`, `gene/compounds`, and the restated ones in `flat/crafting` and `gene/synthesis`. The press ratio is content (`extract` on a `synthesis`: what a pressing gives, or what pressing a dose takes), and the "Steady states" arithmetic in `ideas.md` follows it. Pieces 6 to 8 and queuing several are built and checked as well. A secondary click on a recipe queues three and a quick click as many as can be paid for (`flat/several`). What a cancelled job gives back goes through the same path as anything gained, so what does not fit is said in a note (`flat/refund`). A stack of several strains is drawn as a bundle: a band of one mark per strain in its cell, "a bundle of n kinds" in its name, and a tooltip that lists each run with its mark, its gene strip and its count (view `bundle`, rendered and looked at; a view names the control the cursor rests on with `world.hover`). The queue is in the save: every job with what it holds, every recipe step by its recipe's name with what it took and the time it has left, and the plugin's own work through its save section, each entry carrying its place in the queue; a stack carried between slots is saved and is back in the inventory on loading; a job whose recipe the loaded content no longer defines is given back whole (`save/queue`, `save/lost-recipe`). `SAVE_FORMAT` is 13. Not exercised: the right and crouch clicks in the window and the browser, which only the front ends map |
| N. Keeping the record straight | N2 done and checked (the `deep` digest line). N1, N3, N4 ongoing |

Built on 2026-11-12 outside the packages, at the owner's request, and checked by section 4:

- **Connectors fit their pair.** A sixth style, `paired`, dresses each half of a connector by its own ground (`dress` in `connector-ground`: masonry, trunk, stairwell, shelves), switching where gravity flips. It has weight 250 against the old styles' 100 in every pair and is what an unlisted pair gets. Rigs `pairedcity`, `trippaired`, `trippairedwild`.
- **Sinkholes open to the sky.** `Api.probeRelief(site)` (ABI 29) gives the terrain's height over the tile's ground. A rolled sinkhole exists only where the land is within 8 cells below and 6 above the plane, is cut up to the real surface, its stair reaches the rim, and standing stones ring it, one lit.
- **Three abstract worlds,** `terraces`, `garden` and `trellis` in `plugins/skies`, on a bare ground. They are registered as rising kinds with `bridge: none` and a share of 0, so they do not rise yet: at a share of 0.2 together with ports at 5 in 100 the `live/` flights ran about a twentieth slower, and sinkholes at 5 in 100 cost about as much again. The owner has the prices and has not chosen.
- **A finding for E.** Building the hidden stair for every no-bridge pairing (a call in `connect`) stopped the world at times in `live/far` and `live/fly` with "paints ground that is already built", four runs in six, and never in eight runs without it. The mechanism was not found. It has to be found before E is built.
- **A shortcut in rise discovery:** a vertex whose roll lands on a kind that cannot rise from its ground no longer probes its four neighbors. Both digest lines unchanged by it.

Built on 2026-11-13 for speed, outside the packages, and checked by section 4 (132 tests, `content-check` 0 problems, `check-repaint` with `differing=0` on all ten worlds, `check-forget` on lattice, both digest lines as they were):

- **The digest and the benches follow `--worldgen`.** `world-digest`, `bench-gen` and `bench-drive` are of the world `--worldgen` names, the terrain world if none. `bench-gen` ends with the blueprint's tiles, lines and brushes, and `bench-drive` runs until no node waits on a building (it could stop a few chunks short).
