The planet's lines: `digest populated=10140 materials bc020fbe5a070c6dc3f86d4e9c5b2793 occupancy 68d25ae4b44eaed4304bb90766af2fb2` and `deep populated=856 materials 91d750998318faab1b2dc14d7ddd2acd occupancy 1d4f012431ea9e2aa7953ae1c85d3310`; the first line moved with the connector near spawn, and it had stayed the same across the refactors before that (`7869de27...`), which is the check that they changed nothing. The `live/` tests 3.2, 2.6, 28.1 and 23.9 s; the bench without the profiler, five runs, 3.57 to 3.67 s to load in the two read, the flight 7 to 10 late frames, no mesh short of faces in any of the five. No old binary was kept to run beside it, so what the round's last pieces cost the load is not settled.
- **A review, and `abstraction.md`.** At the owner's asking an Opus agent read the engine, the API and the plugins, wrote `abstraction.md` (eleven ranked candidates for systems: one query for how an edge lies, engine-defined ownership of shared features, tiles that run once per vertex or foot, paint contracts the engine checks, several edge tiles per world, and six more) and made style cleanups in eight plugin files (bounds renamed to `MOST_` and `LEAST_`, imports ordered and merged, `caves.facedVertex` on `turtle.ownerAround`, `vaults.drifted` on `turtle.clamped`). Those were built and checked with everything above. It also noted, unconfirmed: the city builds its foot connector from a quarter and not from the foot; `turtle.roll` has a hundred steps, so odds finer than 1 in 100 round; two lattice settings named odds are thresholds.
- **Which of its candidates are built, settled by a fresh agent as the owner's rules ask.** Built now, all plugin-side, no ABI change and no digest change meant: tile data read through one null-safe template (`dataOf!T` in `plugins/api/api.d`; the wild, city, backrooms, vaults, rooms, palace and planet tiles use it, and the two `connectionOf` copies are gone; the tiles of blocks, skies, library, caves and lattice still cast by hand); the small folds (the angle constants in `turtle.d` for the connector and the tree, one `ring` in the connector, `probeOwnsFoot` asking its face hash once, import order in the connector, horospheres and `tiles.d`, `isLevel` and one `skywardOf` in the planet in place of three inlined copies); and the first stage of the edge query (`Lie` and `lieOf` in `turtle.d`: the planet asks the two pen heights of an edge once per ground and hands them to its voids, shelter, road, trail and standing passes, where it asked five times; caves and lattice still ask for themselves). Deferred, in the order to build them: the per-task memo of site queries (speed work, only if a bench still shows repeated queries); then one ABI bump for several edge tiles per world, the edge query filled by the engine, and engine-defined ownership; then one ABI bump for vertex and foot tiles with paint contracts (moves the digest on purpose, since the connector's paint order changes); then a digest-only package unifying the slacks and the rolls, world by world; then one ABI bump for grounds in content and registered building forms, with cave themes as content last. Each deferred one waits because it changes the plugin ABI, moves a world's digest, or reworks `tiles.d`, which was only just made sound.
- **The tree connector is drawn in brushes again, round.** The pen-drawn dress described in the next entry was taken out. After the fault was fixed, three bench runs in nine ended the flight with one or two chunks drawing fewer faces than their voxels have. A pen stroke is not held to a tile's column: a bough or a clump of leaves reaches nodes beside the connector's line, which never ask for its tile; such a node's chunk can be generated before the stroke exists and regenerated after it, and then its mesh is stale (inferred from which change made it come and go, not traced to a chunk). The engine refuses a brush that does this and checks nothing for a stroke. The roots, boughs and crown are now discs and stacked ellipsoids filled into air inside the column (`disc`, `blob`, `discRows`): six tapering roots, a bough every six cells in a golden-angle spiral with a twig and a tip of leaves, a crown of one ellipsoid and six lobes at uneven heights. Five bench runs in five then ended with no mesh short of faces, loading in 3.57 to 3.67 s with 7 to 10 late frames. View `trunkfoot`, rendered: it still reads as a tree. The same exposure remains for the one spawn tree (`horospheres/tree.d`), which is an upright ground whose tiles the nodes around its column do ask for.
- **What is alive is drawn round.** The owner: the plants and the things meant to be alive, the tree connector most, were square and box-ish. The trunk connector's dress is now drawn with the pen where a tile can use it: a round trunk (`ringRows`, radius 5.5) with dark furrows in five irregular wedges, six roots that dip and wander into the ground, a bough every six cells up in a golden-angle spiral, each a tapering limb that wanders and turns up with a twig of leaves and a cluster at its tip, and a crown of a leafy heart and six lobes at uneven heights (`trunk`, `roots`, `bough`, `crown` in `connector.d`). Strokes are generated before brushes, so the stair's core, the doors and the approach still cut through them. Each pen starts from the tower moved up its column by whole edges (`penAt`): a pen moved a whole connector's height from the foot gives frames whose entries are near cosh of ten units, which the float stroke tests cannot measure against, and the full test run hung twice at a connector trip until then (the cause inferred from the code, not traced). The terrain trees' leaves are ellipsoids whose outer shell drops a fixed one cell in three (`isInBlob` in `plugins/base/forms.d`): round, broad, tall and the weeping tree's head; the spruce's tiers are discs no wider than the reach, so they are not cut square. Views `trunkfoot`, `trunkaside`, `terrain`, rendered.

### After the owner played the build of 2026-11-14

The owner's words, then what each is taken to mean (a **Reading**) and its state.

- "The road generates weird ... Not roads. Trails if you must... not... this shit". And: "The garden world is *not* to have roads or buildings like that... so stop doing those weird shitty wooden thnigs with no windows and horribly stuffy design." Reading: the default world has no roads at all and no timber huts. **Done:** the highways, the road structure at spawn, the shelters and the hamlets are gone from the planet; the stone watchtowers, the crowns and the palaces stay. The `road` debug structure still exists as a scene.
- "the flower-tree thing has floating pedals". Reading: a tree whose trunk a road or trail had cut away, leaving the top in the air. **Done:** nothing clears above a trail any more; a trail only turns the ground's own top cells to gravel (`replace`), so it follows the land and cuts no tree.
- "The 'caves' are often closed and not caves but one kind of boring staircase" and "There are *still* too few cave enterances". **Started:** sinkholes are rolled at 12 vertices in 100, twice as many. The wells on wild ground that end in one closed chamber are not yet changed.
- "Saves need to use a file name that handles versions, as not writing them is not something to ever do!" **Done:** a save's name carries the save format, the worldgen version and the plugin ABI (`saves/<world>-<mode>.<format>.<worldgen>.<abi>.save`), so a new version writes its own file and an old file is neither refused nor overwritten.

Like a community library with a dense basemement and little rooms for activities and such. Open-areas are good but shouldn't be everything." Not built.

- On the hanging maze, which settles question 6 of section 8 and G6: "the hanging maze should *not* have walls that'd ruin it big time. It's not curtains, It's rather flat with some hanging supports. That can be it's own space with it's own subliminal nature. Similar to below the library's asteroid world but without the asteroids. Meaning the asteroid world should, too, have floating lights". Reading: a void layer of flat walkways along the honeycomb's level edges, hung by supports from above, with small lights scattered below it; the same lights under the library's asteroids. Not built.
- **2026-11-14, the lights, the walkways and the library's void, built.** In `plugins/lattice/lattice.d`, all decided from hashes of the edge or vertex:
  - *Stars* (`stars`): one cell of `base:light-panel`, or two in a row, at a hash-chosen cell of an open edge's column, on `star-odds` (1 in 10) of edges. They are in the planet's walkway, island and fractal layers and everywhere in the library's void. View `stars`, rendered: small white points all around in the dark, among the pale shards of the layers there.
  - *Walkways* (`probeWalkway`, `walkway`, `support`): a level edge (its two ends within `level-slack` of a cell of the same height) between `lattice-layer` and the new `maze-layer` edges below the underside is a plank deck four cells wide with spruce rafters under it every six cells, and nothing at its sides. The test is symmetric in the edge's ends (mean height, the deeper crust of the two), so the edge going up from a deck's end can ask it too: on `support-odds` of those it paints a spruce post up one edge, and on `climb-odds` of the posts a stair around it. The planet's layers below the underside are now the lattice to 3 edges, the walkways to 5, the islands to 7 and the fractal past that (`maze-layer`, `fractal-layer` in `plugins/planet/settings.conf`). View `maze`, rendered: one deck, an edge long, with its post going up, alone among stars and rock. That is what the honeycomb gives, as far as the render and the geometry say (inferred, not counted): below the ground plane an edge is level only where a mirror plane standing upright on the ground cuts it in half (the bottom face of each cell stacked under a ground face, and some others), so level edges come one or a ring of five at a time, far apart, and never as a grid. A flat sheet at a fixed depth would need a paint the engine does not have (a box kept to a band of heights); not built.
  - *The library's void* (`bookVoid`, `shelves`, `asteroids`): the planet gives the library ground a crust of `library-crust` (1.5 edges, the same for its wells so they can ask where it ends) and a bottom at `library-abyss` (9). Under it, to `shelf-layer` (3) edges below the underside, a remix of the lattice: girders are shelves (a deck of plank, marble or red carpet on top, two cells of spruce shelf below with runs of books from the library's colours between dividers, end caps, a lamp under one in three), upright edges are bookcases with books on four sides and a stair around one in four. Below that, asteroids: at `asteroid-odds` of vertices, on the line through the vertex that stands most upright, a rounded lump of rock 3 to 10 cells across, stacked octagons (the island's), flatter above than below, painted half by each edge of that line. Stars all through it. View `libraryvoid`, rendered: rounded stepped lumps of grey and dark rock of many sizes floating with stars between them.

In the library world, which has no crust, `probeCrust` is infinite and the well is as before (its world digest is below). View `wellbottom`, rendered: the white marble spiral winding down around a bookcase on its axis to a red carpet landing, a shelf girder running off from it, shelves above, asteroids and stars around.

Giving it one needs an edge tile, which every edge under its floor would then run; that cost was not taken without asking.

Checked by the main session after the void work above: `wellbottom` rendered shows the library's marble spiral ending on a red landing in the open, a plank shelf-girder leading off it, bookcase pillars, rounded rocks and stars; `maze` rendered shows one plank walkway an edge long with a post and stair at its end, alone among islands and rock. So the hanging walkways are **not** what the owner described: they are flat but isolated, because under a ground the honeycomb's edges are level only one or a few at a time. A connected flat sheet of walkways needs a ground that is a plane hanging below its parent (a bare kind that sinks instead of rising), whose face tiles lay walkways along its own edges; the engine has planes that rise and columns that sink, not planes that sink. That is an engine package (how such a plane is rolled, found by nodes under the crust, and left out of gravity and fill), not a question for the owner.

Two costs to weigh, both measured by the builder on the tree before its own edits: with sinkholes at 12 in 100 the load reads 3.96 to 4.10 s where it read 3.57 to 3.67 at 6 in 100, and `live/far` 30.6 s where it read 28.1; and one bench run in three still ended with 5 chunks drawing fewer faces than they should, so that fault was made rarer by taking the pens out of the connector, not removed. The spawn tree still draws with pens.

The rooms are content (`library-room` and `library-piece` in `library.decl`, the list `rooms` on `library:materials`): a weight, `main` or `basement`, wall, floor and lamp, `stair: down`, and pieces that are boxes in the room (a piece with no material is books in the library's colours), so a mod adds a room by adding a node. Main floor (the stories from the landing up, `room-odds` 0.4): reading room, children's corner, meeting room behind glass, carrels, office, narrow stacks, a stair down. Basement (the two stories below the landing, `basement-room-odds` 0.6): storage, boiler room with a lava firebox behind glass, archive of steel shelving, deep stacks, a stair to the story below. A stair room cuts a straight flight of five treads through its floor into the story under it. Each edge between halls has, on `passage-odds` (0.7) of its hash, a passage through the wall at the edge's midpoint, four cells wide and four high over a red carpet runner at level 9, the same height on both sides; a gallery's floor is at level 8, 9 or 10, so it is at most a step up or down into it. No test walks through one. Every built hall now has something under it, chosen from the hash that chose the crypt: the crypt as before (`crypt-odds` 0.33), a cistern (`cistern-odds` 0.34: water a cell deep, an inlaid ledge round the walls and five walkways from the stair's ledge to the centre, columns of the accent stone with lamps at their heads), or an undercroft (columns of the rib stone with lamps at their heads, a dais in the middle with a brazier of lava; a grid of ribs under the hall floor was built and taken out again for its brushes, see the bench below). A port on an open bed now sinks a well two edges to a chamber, as under the built grounds, where its relief lies between -1 and 6 cells (`isWellMouth`, asked both by the well and by the chamber, so they agree). The shaft above the vertex is cleared to the ground plus three cells, and the shaft is lined in grey brick where it cuts soil or rock above the vertex (`wellMouth`) and for the first edge below it, not the second, so the lining does not run on into the chamber (`liningOf`). Half the wells (`square-well-odds`) are a square stair of spruce round an open core four cells wide, the rest the limestone spiral round a newel. The chamber at the bottom joins the caves around it and does not make any: under an open bed a well's chamber does not open the vertices below it (`opennessUnder` passes over it), and it links only to neighbours that are open on their own, below, or above by `holds`. Under the built grounds the well code takes the same paths as before (the spiral, the same lining), but no digest of those worlds was taken before the change, so that they came out the same is not shown. The caves code asks for the edge's lie once per tile now (`lieOf`) and hands it to the well, the drain and the mouth.

The `live/` tests 3.5, 2.5, 31.0 and 24.3 s.

Three runs with everything above and the undercroft's ribs: 4.13, 4.01 and 4.07 s, 9, 5 and 8 late frames, none short. Three runs after the ribs came out: 4.02, 4.05 and 4.14 s, 1, 7 and 4 late frames, the second run ending with 2 meshes short of faces (the old intermittent fault, `draws 0 faces, should draw 171`). So the load reads about 0.1 s slower on average, inside the spread of single runs but on the slow side in every comparison; the brushes held after the load went from 3.23 to 3.34 million to 3.57 to 3.82 million, nearly all in far tiles of risen grounds (the shells past the nearby set), which are the vaults' halls, each now with a room under it, and the library's faces. The next things to cut if the slowdown stands: the room under every vault hall (back to one hall in three, as the crypt was), then the library's rooms (`room-odds`, `basement-room-odds`).
- **2026-11-14, the meshes short of faces, traced and fixed.** The guess that pen strokes caused it was wrong. A temporary record per chunk (when it arrived fresh or restored, was dropped, was unloaded with its node, was queued, was meshed and with how many faces) showed two ways a chunk ends up drawing fewer faces than its voxels have, and no case of a restored chunk whose voxels differed from the ones meshed:
  - *A uniform chunk beside a dropped one.* A chunk filled with one material (air, water) arrived fresh while its dense neighbour was dropped. `needsMesh` decides for a uniform chunk from its populated neighbours, so it saw none to show against and the chunk was never queued; the neighbour was restored the next frame, and a restored arrival queued none of its neighbours, on the reasoning that their meshes were made while it was there. A dense chunk does not have the problem, since the mesher waits for a dropped neighbour to come back before it meshes. A restored arrival now queues its populated neighbours that are uniform (`settle` in `arrivals.d`); a uniform chunk meshes in a few rows, so the cost is small.
  - *A node unloaded while one of its chunks was being restored.* `unloadNode` released the node's settled chunks and kept the one in flight, but told the renderer the whole block was gone, which dropped every corner's mesh; the restore then arrived and, being a restore, was never meshed again (`draws 0 faces, should draw 133`, several chunks of one node at once). A node with a chunk still generating is now left alone until the sweep comes back to it (`unloadNode` in `eviction.d`); it is pinned against eviction for the same reason already. The loop that queued the node's populated chunks after a partial unload is gone with it: no chunk was ever populated there. `isResident` was in `app/main.d` and is now one function in `eviction.d`, which `isSettled` uses as well.
  - Checked: the bench without the profiler, three runs before the first edit: 4.00, 4.13 and 4.16 s to load with 10, 10 and 11 late frames of 1800, and 2, 1 and 0 meshes short of faces after the flight. With the first fix alone one run in five still ended with 2 short. With both, eight runs in a row: 4.06, 4.16, 3.96, 4.10, 4.01, 4.04, 4.03 and 3.90 s, with 8, 8, 12, 7, 10, 8, 7 and 8 late frames, and none short after the load or after the flight. 139 tests pass, `check-forget` on lattice `differing=0`. The meshes that draw more faces than they should after a flight (faces against a node that was unloaded) are as before.
- **2026-11-14, the hanging maze, built as a ground that hangs.** The isolated walkways are gone; the maze is a plane of its own.
  - *The engine (plugin ABI 36).* A new form, `hanging`, and `api.hanging(odds, under, edges, tag, tile, data, size)` at the end of `Api`, which gives a bare kind with the number of edges it hangs and the tag of the ground it hangs under. Only the root hangs one, since a node asks for a ground's rising list only within `RISE_REACH` of that ground, except the root's, which every node asks for. It reuses the rising machinery with the tower going down: the root rolls it per vertex like a rising kind (`rolled` gives it the kind's own edges), `risenHorosphere` turns the tower down instead of up, and the plane is the one perpendicular to the vertical line that many edges under the vertex, facing up toward the root. It fills nothing (bare), nothing rises from it (`canRiseFrom`, `discoverHorospheres`), it flattens no relief (`flatLinesNear`, `reliefShareAmong`), it is not measured and has no foot line, so no connector. A node skips it without carrying it along a path when the root lies too close above for the plane to be within `RISE_REACH` (`isAboveHangingReach`), so the nodes near the surface carry no path down to it; they still make it, once, when its vertex is in their ground's ring, which walks the line down its thirteen edges; a node asks for its face tile when it is nearer to it than to the root, give or take the same margin a risen ground uses (`probeRisenSide`). It takes no part in the weighing of grounds for gravity; within `HANGING_HOLD` (0.35) over its plane it turns gravity to its own up, fading over `HANGING_FADE` (0.1) at both ends (`heldByHanging`), so a body standing on it walks it and one that falls off falls on under the root's pull, which a unit from the maze's foot is already tilted about fifty degrees from the maze's up (worked out from the geometry, not measured). A ground without a fill may now be given a bottom with `api.abyss` (the rule that the bottom lies under the crust is kept for grounds that fill), and the maze has one, three edges under it, so the nodes far under it stop asking for tiles again.
  - *The maze (`maze` in `plugins/lattice/lattice.d`, given by the planet).* The planet hangs it under the wild root, thirteen edges down (`maze-edges`, a little under the root's bottom at twelve, so the void's layers end above it and it is its own space), at 4 vertices in 1000 (`maze-odds`), and forces one five edges from spawn (`mazeSiteOf`). The code is in the lattice because the stars, the deck and rafter materials and the void's look are there, and the planet already compiles `lattice.d` in; the planet gives the kind, since it needs the kind's id to force one and to give it its reach and bottom (`hangMaze`), and a kind added after the registry's keeps every other kind's rolls where they were. Each face lays a deck two cells wide on its own side of every edge whose edge hash keeps it (`maze-walk-odds` 0.6), so both faces agree and the walkways run on along the honeycomb's straight lines of edges; on `maze-spoke-odds` (0.3) of edges a spoke runs from the edge's middle to the face's centre, where a small platform joins them; spruce rafters every six cells under the decks along edges; no walls, no railings. At a vertex where any of its four edges is walked, on `maze-support-odds` (0.3), a post two cells square goes up three edges into the dark. Under every quarter, two rolls of `maze-star-odds` (0.6) put a light panel, or two, between 6 cells and two edges below. The tile reaches three edges up and two down. The void's layers are back as they were before the walkways: the lattice to 3 edges under the underside, islands to 5, the fractal below (`fractal-layer` 5), with the stars in the island and fractal layers.
  - *Views.* `maze` sets a body down on a deck a third of an edge along an edge from the forced maze's foot, in walk mode, and checks that after two seconds it stands there under 0.12 over the plane: rendered, tan plank walkways flat in the dark, a dark spruce post going up out of frame beside them, thin posts farther off, and white lights below through the gaps. A first version set the body down on the plank nearest the foot vertex and it fell through the deck; a check there showed gravity was the maze's up and the first solid cell straight down was spruce, so the body was most likely set into the post at the vertex (inferred, not traced); set down along an edge it stands. `mazeabove` looks down at 70 degrees from half a unit over the foot vertex: rendered, a pale cluster of deck strips meeting at the post with separate pieces of deck around it, and darkness past about half a unit; it does not show the grid beyond. From higher up the camera is among the fractal layer's marble limbs, which hide the maze.
  - *Checked on the tree with both jobs:* 139 tests, 176 views, 9 films, `content-check` 0 problems, `check-repaint` `differing=0` on all ten worlds (planet 74146 tiles, city 3037, backrooms 3056, library 2554, vaults 3038, caves 30171, lattice 715410, the skies 1371 each), `check-forget` on lattice, all three programs built. The planet's lines: `digest populated=10140 materials 0f01462cb29c083708698d3a2ddc21cd occupancy 9016ab2679f203779d2c0c8cf3f94be8` and `deep populated=856 materials 20b54e419f36b5ea3dfdeebc173b280f occupancy 1add3f9f5f95d59906f360702d88a4ef`; no digest was taken before, so which change moved them is not shown (the layers moved back and the maze is new; the maze lies below what `deep` samples). The `live/` tests 3.5, 2.6, 30.6 and 24.0 s. The bench without the profiler, three runs after the last edit: 4.12, 4.16 and 4.20 s to load, 9, 7 and 5 late frames of 1800, none short of faces; before the first edit of the round 4.00, 4.13 and 4.16 s with 10, 10 and 11, and with job 1 alone eight runs from 3.90 to 4.16 s. The load reads about 0.06 s slower on average than before the round, inside the spread of single runs and not settled; the late frames are fewer. Not built: a law that a body stands on the maze (the view `maze` checks it), mazes under risen grounds (only the root hangs one), and a view that shows the grid from farther off.

- **2026-11-14, the wells' stairs made one flight, and deeper.** All plugin-side (`plugins/caves/caves.d`, one line in `plugins/planet/plugin.d`), no ABI change.
  - *What was wrong.* Not half a turn: the second edge's stretch wound the other way. Each edge's tile took the stair's frame from `settled(mouth)`, which picks the roll of the mouth frame whose face hash is least; that fixes the frame's x and z but not y, and the tiles of consecutive edges see the line in frames of either handedness, so the next stretch could come out mirrored. A new test reads the stair cell by cell down the shaft and checks that the tread moves on round by one step the same way every cell (`structure/wellflight` and its city, vaults and wild copies). On the tree as it was, the wild well at the first port from spawn broke first 25 cells down, the first cell of the second edge, and every step of that edge after it; the city well broke from 26 cells down the same way (and also at 6, see the subway below).
  - *The fix* (`flightAt`, `wound`, `stairDown`). The flight's frame is still the least-hash roll of the mouth, and its handedness now comes from the faces too: y points toward whichever of the two neighbouring faces has the lower hash, and a stretch painted in a frame of the other hand has its treads' y mirrored. The tread's phase is counted from the mouth (`above * edge` cells already descended), so it would stay continuous even if an edge's length were not a multiple of the treads in a turn. After it: `structure/wellflight` (backrooms, 72 cells, two joints), `vaultwellflight` (72), `wildwellflight` (48, as far as the rock goes at that port) and, once the subway kept out of the shaft, `citywellflight` (72) all read no broken step.
  - *Walked.* Rig `wellstair` (caves plugin): a solid ground whose port rule takes every vertex, with route stops laid on every tread of the well at the vertex next to the root, three edges down, from the same `flightAt`. `structure/wellstair` follows them on foot: all 72 stops, 2.95 edges down, standing on the limestone stair.
  - *Deeper* (`wellDepthOf`, `probeWell`, `isOpenedBy`). A well goes down to the rock's end under its mouth, rounded up to a whole edge and at least two (`probeCrust` at the mouth), and ends there on a landing of its stair's material twelve cells square (`landing`); where the rock goes deeper than the cave band, or there is no crust (city, backrooms), it goes the whole band (5 edges under a root ground, 3 under a risen one) and ends in a chamber. Under a sealed ground every vertex the well passes from the sealed top down is a chamber of full size, each of which opens caves around it as the old depth-2 chamber did; under an open ground only the bottom one is forced open and the well passes through whatever chambers are open on their own. A chamber on a well keeps the well's column above and below it, the caves lay no shaft tube along the well's own edges, and the drain is not painted on a well's edge. Linings stay in the first two edges (`liningOf`). The probe checks that the line it climbs ends on the ground plane (`isOnPlane`): without it a slanted deep edge passed the old checks and `live/far` stopped on an assert in `turnSite`. The planet's underground tile now paints the void's layers before the caves, so on a well's own edge in the void the stair wins over the lattice; across tiles the order is still the tile hashes', and a lattice joint can stand on a well's landing.
  - *Views, rendered:* `citywellchamber` (2.85 edges under a city port: a granite dome with a tan floor and a lamp, the steel spiral and its newel coming down through it and on through the floor); `wildwelllanding` (the spruce square stair hanging out of the rock's underside among the lattice's grey girders and stars); `wildwellbottom` (looking down the same stair onto a grey-brick lattice joint; the landing is not clearly seen); `citywelljoint` (inside the shaft at the joint: the newel and the treads stepping round it; a render cannot show a joint well from inside an eight-cell shaft, the flight tests are the check); `wildwelljoint` (the same place in the wild well: the spruce treads stepping down along one wall of the shaft and on round the corner, through bands of slate and sandstone, no break). Before the planet's order was changed `wildwelllanding` showed the lattice's plank stair round a pole in place of the well's.
  - *Not built:* a way from a subway or a cave tube into a well's shaft at the depths it passes; a law that a body can walk the wild square stair (only its geometry is checked).

- **2026-11-14, the city's subway.** `tunnel` in `plugins/blocks/blocks.d` is replaced by `subway`, painted by the face that paints the street, all in the street edge's own column (no deeper than twelve cells under the road, which is as far as that column reaches; deeper, the ground under an edge is not a straight run of cells). Everything that crosses into another edge's ground is decided from hashes: a line's depth from the line hash (`depthOf`, `deep-odds` 0.5), a station from the edge hash (`stopOf`, `station-odds` 0.3), its band colour from the line hash, and what lies at each end of an edge from the streets and ports there (`End`, `endAt`).
  - *Track.* Under every street a bore six cells wide: gravel ballast with two steel rails, wet-concrete walls from five cells off each vertex, and one sunlight lamp over the track in the middle of each edge that is not a station, so the bore between stations is dark. A deep line's ballast is twelve cells under the road, a shallow one's six; bores meet where lines of the same depth cross and pass over and under each other where they differ. A line that ends where no street goes on gets a steel buffer at the vertex. At a port, or a vertex where a ground rises or stands its foot, the subway stops five cells short so the well's shaft and the connector are left alone (that is what broke the city well's flight 6 cells down).
  - *Stations* (`hall`, `fittings`, `stationHalf`), cells 6 to 17 of the edge: white brick walls with a band of the line's colour, concrete platforms a cell above the ballast on both sides with a yellow edge (pollen), four steel pillars along the platform edges. A deep station is lit from a plaster ceiling and has a mezzanine of pale tile two cells under the road over its middle eight cells, with a stair from it down to each platform behind a steel rail; a shallow one has lamps along the tops of its walls and its ceiling is the road. Each end of a station has a street entrance (`entrance`): a stair cut down from the sidewalk to the mezzanine (deep) or the platform (shallow), with olivine railings on three sides and a lamp on a post. The two halves are one shape turned half round, so whichever face paints the edge paints the same station.
  - *Crossings* (`transfer`). Where a deep line passes under a shallow one, the deep line's edge at that vertex, if it is not a station, cuts a tiled passage beside its track with an acacia stair of six steps from the shallow bore down to its own; a station end at such a crossing has no street entrance.
  - *Content:* new fields on `blocks:materials` (bore light, ballast, rail, platform, platform edge, station wall and ceiling, pillar, mezzanine, railing, entrance, transfer, bands); `access-odds` and `pipe` are gone. All existing materials.
  - *Tests:* `structure/citytunnel` now states the subway (lands on ballast or a rail below the street); `structure/subwaystation` (on a platform's edge, and the edge lies above the ballast beside it) and `structure/subwaymezzanine` (a mezzanine floor between the street and its platform).
  - *Views, rendered:* `subwaybore` (rails on ballast in a concrete bore under its lamp, a junction's crossing rail and a station's yellow edges and band far ahead); `subwayplatform` (along a shallow station: yellow edges on both sides of the track, pillars, white walls with a brown band, the dark underside of the road for a ceiling); `subwaymezzanine` (pale floor with a lamp set in it, plaster ceiling lamps, red band, the steel rail round a stair opening); `subwayentrance` (from above the street: a hole in the sidewalk with steps going down and olive railings round it; the lamp post is not obvious); `subwaycrossing` (a tiled passage with an acacia landing and steps going down toward a lamp); `citytunnel` (lands on ballast).
  - *Not built:* trains or signals; platforms that run on across an edge (each station is one edge with end walls); entrances on edges that are not stations; a test that walks from the street to a platform or through a transfer. Where two lines of the same depth cross and both edges at the vertex are stations, their entrances can meet at the street corner (a railing post over the other stair) — seen in the geometry, not in a render.
  - *Checked on the tree with both jobs:* 146 tests (139 and seven new), 186 views (ten new), 9 films, `content-check` 0 problems, `check-repaint` `differing=0` on all ten worlds (planet 74142 tiles, city 3037, backrooms 3061, library 2554, vaults 3040, caves 30171, lattice 715410, the skies 1371 each) and on the rig `wellstair`, `check-forget` on lattice, all three programs built. The planet's lines are now `digest populated=10140 materials 371e9eb5bb95fcf2bb544a51cd173089 occupancy d111c6dc21c0a91dc972ef11a579c833` and `deep populated=856 materials 3bbf36aee93e3b13fceb2b9606ab5c17 occupancy b9719d6777933fa27e2b7d40cb18825e` (both moved, from the wells and the void-before-caves order); the city's `digest ... materials cc0e24466f0fac46c3b5de7a36df0109 occupancy a789d4e2aee45181e617ceacd4290b2f` and `deep ... materials 3f2ff5e219a85ee7a23a39a9e81da56c occupancy 805382c23f5f8af45990d9fd41c6198c`; no digests were taken before, so which change moved which is not shown. The `live/` tests 3.6, 2.6, 31.4 and 24.4 s (with job A alone 3.6, 2.6, 31.0 and 24.7). The bench without the profiler, three runs before the first edit: 4.14, 4.02 and 4.06 s to load, 14, 11 and 6 late frames of 1800, no mesh short of faces; job A alone one run 4.15 s; three runs after the last edit: 4.01, 4.17 and 4.14 s, 11, 6 and 14 late frames, none short. About 0.03 s slower on average, inside the spread of single runs and not settled; brushes held after the load 3.57 to 3.70 million against 3.40 to 3.68 million before.

- **2026-11-14, the open items of the round: pole stairs, the subway, the hanging maze.** All plugin-side and in `app/scripts/`, no ABI change, nothing under `source/` changed.
  - *1. Lattice pole stairs* (`spiral` in `plugins/lattice/lattice.d`). A new test, `structure/latticeflight`, finds pole stairs of two edges on the vertical lines through the lattice world's ground vertices and reads the treads a cell at a time up the pole, checking each lies as many 45 degree steps round, the same way, as it lies cells higher (the three cells either side of a joint are skipped: the landing is there, and the grids of the edges meeting at the vertex do not line up cell for cell in the read). On the tree as it was, the first stair found wound one way up its lower edge and the other way up the next (+45 degrees a cell up to the joint, then -45), the same fault the wells had. Fixed the way the wells were: `flightAt` and `wound` (with `Flight` and `Winding`) moved from `caves.d` to `plugins/turtle/turtle.d` and both plugins use them. A pole has no mouth to count from, so a flight is counted from the foot of its run: `spiral` walks down the line from the edge's lower end while the edges below are stairs by the same rule (`isClimbed` for the weave, `isShelfClimbed` for the library's bookcases), at most `MOST_FLIGHT_EDGES` (8), and takes the frame, hand and phase from that foot; an edge whose run goes on further than that paints no treads, so the cut is the same seen from every edge. The edges are taken to be upright all the way down the run (only their hashes are read); in the lattice world they are, under the planet's void a run of the regular weave or of mixed weaves can count from a foot that is not painted, which moves the phase but not the agreement between the edges of a run. Also fixed, found on the way: a pole stair's own edge was decorated at its foot like any upright edge, so a hut's roof could close the stair a few cells up; an edge that is a stair is no longer decorated (`lay`). After the fix the test passes on three separate lines; lattice `check-repaint` and `check-forget` `differing=0`. A test in the default world's void was written and dropped: each probe there generates everything within the world's reach of it (`Game.generateAround` ignores its reach and uses the load reach), and the search ran past the 120 s limit; the void uses the same `spiral`. View `latticestair`, rendered: a steel pole with plank treads winding up it to the plank landing at the joint, beams and stars behind.
  - *2a. Walks in the subway.* Places are found from the city's start by reading cells in the frames of the ground's vertices (`ensureSided` in `app/scripts/walk.d`: a vertex frame and whether the painter's frame is mirrored to it, told apart by where the entrance's railing stands), and walked with `followStops` in `climb.d` (the route follower, for any positions). `structure/subwaywalk`: from the sidewalk down a deep station's entrance, across the mezzanine and down its stair onto a platform, 13 of 13 stops, 12.8 cells down. `structure/subwaytransfer`: from a shallow line's track down the transfer's stair onto the deep line's gravel, 8 of 8 stops. The transfer could not be walked as built: its passage had 3 cells of headroom and the body's head caught the wall over the first step down; it now has 4 (`HEADROOM`).
  - *2b. The corner.* View `subwaycorner` with the rule off, rendered from above the crossing: two railed stair pits at the same street corner, one's lamp post standing against the other's railing. Now, where a station's end is crossed by a line of its own depth with a station beside it, the vertex hash picks which of the two lines keeps its entrances there (`isCornerYielded`, `CORNER_SALT`); rendered again: one pit at the corner, with its lamp.
  - *2c. Headroom.* Shallow lines lie two cells deeper (`SHALLOW_BED` -8) and a shallow platform is two cells over the ballast (`SHALLOW_PLATFORM`): it stands 5 cells under the road with 4 cells clear over it, its entrance has six steps. Traded: where a deep line passes under a shallow one, the deep bore is three cells high for the five cells either side of the vertex (`CROSSED_TOP`), so that the shallow ballast stays over it; elsewhere it is five as before. The transfer has 4 steps. Deep platforms stay one cell over the ballast, so the mezzanine keeps 4 cells over them. `structure/shallowplatform`: 4 cells clear, 2 over the ballast, a body set on it stands and walks its length (0.36 units, past the pillars). View `shallowplatform`, rendered: the platform running on to its end wall between white walls with a brown band and lamps, steel pillars, the yellow edge, the road's dark underside for a ceiling.
  - *2d. Stations that continue.* Where the next edge of the line beyond a vertex is also a station (its edge hash), the street goes on, the end is not a port or a foot, and no line of another depth crosses there, the hall, the platforms and their edges run to the vertex with no end wall (`isContinued`, `stationOf`, `insideOf`), and the station on the other side does the same, so stations are one or several edges long. Mezzanines and entrances stay where they were in each edge. View `subwaycontinued`, rendered: a long hall with yellow edges on both platforms running past one mezzanine into the next stretch.
  - *2e. The subway and the wells.* Where a line ends five cells short of a port, a doorway four cells high is cut through the last cell before the shaft (`wellDoor`), level with a tread of the well's stair: the city works out which tread faces the doorway from the same `flightAt` the well uses and the shared tread shape (`wellTreadAt`, `WELL_TREADS` in `turtle.d`), finds the depth where that tread passes, the nearest the column can reach (no deeper than cell -12, no higher than 5 under the road), and cuts steps from the track up or down to it. The city paints only cells outside the shaft (the doorway is the cell next to it, and the well's lining starts 12 cells under the road, below every bore); the cave tile paints only the shaft, so no cell is painted by both. Only lines that are not stations at that end get a doorway. `structure/subwaywell`: from the track, through the doorway (11 cells under the road there) onto a steel tread, 5 of 5 stops, no drop. A first cut 3 cells high caught the body's head the same way the transfer did. View `subwaywell`, rendered: down the track between the rails to the bore's end, where the opening drops into the shaft and the steel stair shows through it.
  - *2f.* The entrance railings are steel. The view `subwayentrance` searched for olivine and now finds an entrance by its railing; rendered: a railed pit on the sidewalk with its lamp, the yellow edge of the platform glimpsed far down.
  - *3. The hanging maze.* `structure/maze` (claims): a body set on a walkway stands 0.046 over the maze's plane; it walks one from beside the foot vertex to beside the vertex two edges on, 1.96 edges, without a single step off the ground; walking off the side, a second later it is 1.24 under the plane. View `mazeside`, from the side at walkway height: walkways in a broken grid, spruce posts at several vertices, lights below through the gaps, a pale limb of the layer above; several faces of walkways at once, which the view from above did not show. The maze helpers moved from `views.d` to `walk.d`.
  - *3, not built: mazes under raised grounds,* because it is not a small change. What stands in the way: `canRiseFrom` (`horospheres.d`) lets a hanging kind hang only from the root; that alone is one line. But `discoverHorospheres` asks for a raised ground's list of what rises from it (`addRisingOver`) only from nodes within `RISE_REACH` (7) of its plane (`isWithinRisingReach`), while a maze hangs `maze-edges` (13) edges, about 13.8, under its ground, so no node near such a maze would ever find it. Widening that band for grounds that have a hanging kind under them means every node in a band 14 units deep under every such ground walks up to its ground node by node (`ensureGroundNode`) to ask: a cost to every load under raised wild grounds, not measured. And there is no room for it: raised grounds stand 2 to 4 edges over the root (`LOWEST_RISE_EDGES`, `RISE_EDGE_CHOICES`), so 13 edges under one is 9 to 11 edges under the root's ground, inside the root's own crust (5.6 thick, 2 thin) and the void layers the planet paints under it (lattice, islands, the fractal down to the root's bottom at 12), where the maze, which only fills air, would be cut up by rock and clutter. A maze there needs a hang depth that clears the root's layers or a place for it the root does not fill, which is a question of what the planet looks like under its raised grounds, not of the engine.
  - *Checked:* 152 tests (146 and six new: `latticeflight`, `subwaywalk`, `subwaytransfer`, `shallowplatform`, `subwaywell`, `maze`), 192 views (six new: `latticestair`, `subwaycorner`, `shallowplatform`, `subwaywell`, `subwaycontinued`, `mazeside`), 9 films, `content-check` 0 problems, `check-repaint` `differing=0` on all ten worlds (planet 74142 tiles, city 3037, backrooms 3061, library 2554, vaults 3040, caves 30171, lattice 715410, the skies 1371 each), `check-forget` on lattice `differing=0`, all three programs built. Digests now: planet `digest populated=10140 materials 3ce680136d06a812f436abe44d768cf2 occupancy 97b4e1059dd4c60d7330e864a9c7457d` and `deep populated=856 materials 13b20451afe50eaf7b9a791739de39c0 occupancy 6b70f72354e30317fee4b0382c11cb5a` (both moved: the void's pole stairs and the risen cities' subways); city `digest ... materials 2c466b569fb1312cc63abebdcedf88f2 occupancy be6126fb4d8ef7aa6b8ca26cffa27732` and `deep ... materials f9295798bb5d82040c45470377eaed64 occupancy 5cd016e00d32f91fed4f50b910c13375`; lattice `digest ... materials 845a06471b91300489d56bb7ce761b7a occupancy 9707baa8419c788e74170e0c34005d86` and `deep ... materials ba62d2a4247ceed5513efc7785e90931 occupancy 3a356b31df9ca15db07a2980b27e49df`; no digests were taken before. The `live/` tests 3.6, 2.6, 31.4 and 24.2 s. The bench without the profiler, three runs before the first edit: 4.18, 4.17 and 4.18 s to load, 8, 9 and 8 late frames of 1800, no mesh short of faces; three after the last: 4.05, 4.11 and 4.00 s, 7, 10 and 5 late frames, none short. Brushes held after the load 3.56 to 3.71 million against 3.42 to 3.55 million before. `bench-gen` and `bench-drive` were not run.

- **2026-11-14, the round's leftovers: the continued station, the deep bore, the void's pole stairs, the maze's lights, and mazes under raised grounds closed.** Plugin-side (`plugins/blocks/blocks.d`, `plugins/lattice/lattice.d`), one method split in `plugins/game/game.d`, the rest in `app/scripts/`. No ABI change, nothing under `source/` changed.
  - *1. The slabs in `subwaycontinued`.* Not a platform end or a stair. The station the view found was a deep one that continued across a vertex where another deep line crossed it (`isContinued` allowed a crossing line of the same depth). The hall and the crossing bore then took the same cells, and which won was the order of the tiles' hashes: read cell by cell at that joint, one quarter of the station had its platform and yellow edge cut away for three cells with the crossing line's steel rail lying across half the track bed, and the other three quarters walled the crossing line off with the station's wall and platforms. A station now continues only across a vertex that no line crosses (`!end.isCrossed()`); at a crossing each station keeps its end walls and the bores meet in the plain stretch as they did before stations continued. New test `structure/subwaythrough`: at the first continued joint from the city's start, nothing but the line's own two rails stands in the four cells over the ballast for ten cells either side, both platform edges are whole for four cells either side (the entrances' stairs start past that and, in a shallow station, stand on the edge), and a body walks the track bed through, 2 of 2 stops. On the tree as it was it failed with 1 cell blocked and 3 edge cells missing. The view now looks along the track from over its middle; rendered after the fix: a shallow station's track running clear between two yellow edges into the next stretch. Seen in that render and not changed: a shallow hall is ten cells wide and the road with its sidewalks eight, so the outer cell of its ceiling each side is the lot's ground, a green strip where the lot is a park.
  - *2. The deep bore under a shallow line.* New test `structure/subwayunder`: at a transfer whose deep line goes on past the vertex, 3 cells are clear over the deep ballast under the shallow line's ballast, and a body walks the deep track bed from ten cells before the vertex to ten cells past it, 2 of 2 stops. Three cells are enough on the level; the transfer caught the head where the floor stepped down. Nothing changed in the subway for it.
  - *3. Pole stairs in the default world's void.* `structure/voidflight`: on the lines down from the ground's vertices, edges 3 to 9 below the ground, the first stair of two edges (vertex 170 of the search, edges 5 and 4 below) reads as one flight, 32 cells, none off, in 2.7 s. The earlier attempt did not run long because of the reach: a column spot some two hundred cells along a line from one site makes `collectChunksNear` never end (two runs past 120 s stopped inside it, with as few as four vertices searched; why the position is bad was not traced). The search and the read now move the site along the line by whole edges and address cells within two edges of it (`stairPairOn`, `checkPoleFlights`). `isNearJoint` also took a C remainder of a negative cell. `Game.generateAround` did generate the game's touch reach (0.5, the `reach` of the config, not a load radius) around the place whatever it was given, then the region asked for. It is now `generateWithin`, which generates what lies within the reach given; the step and the settle call `generateAroundBody` (the touch reach, then the body's region), which is what they did before. Every script caller passes what it reads: `ensureSided` 0.5 (it passed 0 and read cells an edge away), the tread reader six cells, `isDeckAt` a cell.
  - *4. The maze's lights* (`mazeStar`). Each quarter rolls six strings (`maze-star-odds` 0.6 each) in place of two single lights: a string is one brush, a column one cell wide from 12 to 35 cells under the plane with a light every 5, 7 or 9 cells, so about thirteen lights a quarter at many depths from under four brushes, where there were about one and a half from one. Deeper than a unit and a half the columns under neighbouring vertices stand apart (space widens downward) and the lights read as clumps with dark between, which a band from one to two edges down showed; closer spacing along a string reads as streaks. The tile's reach below is now 36 cells, where it was two edges. `base:light-panel` glows and is in no hidden family (`base.content`). Views `mazeside` (before: three or four dots through the gaps; after: dozens of points of several sizes), `maze` and the new `mazelights` (from eight cells over the decks looking down: lights all through the gaps), rendered. The lights are solid, as they were: a body that falls off a walkway can land on one, and with nine times the lights it will more often (not counted; the test's fall passes them and is 1.24 under the plane after a second).
  - *5. Mazes under raised grounds: closed, not built.* Checked in the code: only the root hangs one (`canRiseFrom`); a raised ground's rising list is asked only within `RISE_REACH` (7) of its plane (`discoverHorospheres`, `isWithinRisingReach`); raised grounds stand 2, 3 or 4 edges over the root (`LOWEST_RISE_EDGES`, `RISE_EDGE_CHOICES`). The planet gives the raised wild and vault grounds a crust of 3.4 edges and a bottom at 9, the library 1.5 and 9, and the root a crust of 5.6 (2 where thin), its void's layers under that and its bottom at 12. So under a raised ground there is no space of its own: its crust ends between 1.4 edges under the root's plane and 0.6 over it, and everything below that is the root's ground, rock and void. A hang short enough to be found (6.6 edges at most) puts the maze 2.6 to 4.6 edges under the root's plane: in the root's rock where the crust is thick, in the root's lattice layer where it is thin, and the maze fills only air. Under a library that rose four edges there are 2.5 edges of air over the root's ground, which the library's shelves and asteroids already fill. There is no place where it would be seen and not cut up, so it is not open work. It would become possible only if a raised ground stood higher over the root than its crust and a hang together.
  - *Checked:* 155 tests (152 and three new), 193 views (one new), 9 films, `content-check` 0 problems, `check-repaint` `differing=0` on all ten worlds (planet 74142 tiles, city 3037, backrooms 3061, library 2554, vaults 3040, caves 30171, lattice 715410, the skies 1371 each), `check-forget` on lattice `differing=0`, all three programs built. Digests taken before the first edit and after the last: the planet's two lines and the lattice's two are the same (the stretch sampled holds no continued station at a crossing, and the maze lies outside both samples); the city's moved, `digest ... materials a0fe958b5ea233428e4482a3e611c214 occupancy 81db997cf81a231011fb6b820ce996cd` and `deep ... materials a5b8d563d755dca70d8419b34e10aa05 occupancy 57bfce12a65a9cd5dfefa499dd8be6da`, from the stations that no longer continue across a crossing. The `live/` tests 3.7, 2.6, 31.0 and 24.3 s (before 3.7, 2.6, 31.4 and 24.2). The bench without the profiler, three runs before the first edit: 4.42, 4.08 and 4.01 s to load, 12, 9 and 17 late frames of 1800, no mesh short of faces; three after the last: 4.15, 4.10 and 3.99 s, 8, 11 and 10 late frames, none short. `bench-gen` params 1719.3, 1740.1 and 1721.3 ms before, 1726.2, 1720.9 and 1714.9 after, the blueprint the same (5738 tiles, 4831 lines, 355339 brushes); `bench-drive` 1538.4, 1512.8 and 1517.3 ms before, 1533.8, 1500.1 and 1491.9 after.

Asked next by the owner, built since (the two entries above):

- "Do the underground staircases connect right? I think there's a 180deg rotation that is missed somehow as it doesn't connect. I'd also like them to keep going generally as opposed to being just a stack of two." Built: the stretches wound opposite ways at the joints and now make one flight; wells go down through the caves to the rock's end or the cave band's bottom.
- "I also want some work on the NYC what I'd assume are subways. Right now they are too minimal and I'd like for them to be more intentional and feel like a subway space. Empty train lines, platforms, a bit of verticality, entrances and so on". Built: a subway in place of the service tunnels, two depths, stations with platforms, mezzanines, street entrances and transfer stairs.

### 2026-11-18: after the owner played again

The owner's words, then what each is taken to mean (a **Reading**) and its state. Benchmark numbers are reported to the owner when they are taken and are not kept here; what is recorded is what was compared with what, and which way it came out.

- "I'm trying to find the ores underground and can't find them. I think a lot of things aren't wired up". Checked, nothing changed: the ores generate (`material-census` counts all three; each is about one cell in 1100 of the rock it can form in), a chunk that was filled with the stand-in material takes its real rock and veins when it is exposed or dug into (`resolveMaterials`, `resolveIfExposed`), and the chain from ore to bar to pickaxe is whole (`content-check`). What makes them hard to find is by design and may be wrong: a vein is one ball of radius 2 in 8 chunks in 100, seen only where a cave or a cut crosses it; copper and iron lie one and two rock bands down; and a block that needs a better pickaxe gives one note and then does not crack. `vein-odds` and `vein-radius` on `base:terrain` are the numbers to turn if ore should be easier to meet.
- "the caves should be more network-ish. I find lots of the spiral entrances but it feels closed and linear." **Built.** A chamber was joined only to the chambers it hangs from (its ways up), and near the surface it has exactly one, so every sinkhole hung a tree of its own and two trees an edge apart never met. An edge between two chambers that are both open is now a passage too, on `cross-odds` (1 in 2) of its edge hash (`isCrossing`, `isLinked` in `caves.d`); the entrance search still follows the tree alone (`isTreeLinked`), since it runs before the world has a root. It only joins chambers that exist, so it costs where a cave is painted anyway. Sinkholes are rolled at 6 vertices in 100 where they were 12 (`root-odds`), and the deep bands keep a little less (`keep-odds`), which is what paid for the passages and for the skies below. Tried first and taken out: opening a chamber in rock beside an open one (a side gallery). It ran for every closed vertex in the rock, where nothing is painted, and the streaming tests were slower with it. **Not shown:** that it feels like a network. Nothing counts loops or joins; the census of B is still the tool that would.
- "I want to be able to get from planet to the others. There needs to be more integration." **Built for the three that could not be reached.** `terraces`, `garden` and `trellis` rise from the default world: each has a share of a third (together one kind's worth, as the vaults have), a reach of what its tile paints (24 cells up, 9 down, worked out from its tallest spire and deepest pit), a built bridge, and pairs with wild ground that lean to the lantern and drift connectors (`plugins/skies`). View `terracesrising` finds one from spawn and fails if none rises; rendered: a lantern tower climbing from wild ground. **Assumed:** that a plain bridge is wanted. An older want of the owner's says the more abstract structures should have no bridges, which is why the vaults are reached only by the hidden stair, and the skies are abstract too; today's want is to be able to get to them. The answer lives in `bridge` on the three `connector-ground` nodes in `plugins/skies/skies.content`: `hidden` makes each a mist stair like the vaults', `none` cuts them off again, and their share is `RISING_SHARE` in `plugins/skies/plugin.d`. The others were already reachable: wild, city and backrooms rise and are bridged, the library and the vaults rise as kinds (the vaults by the hidden stair), the caves lie under all of it and the lattice under that.
- "I'd also like you to try to improve each. Some of them feel *very* same-y", and, when that was put off: "I definately want different cave stacks. That's what I asked for". **Built.** What lies under a ground is that ground's own. A `caves-stack` in the caves' content names a ground by tag and lists layers, each a depth in edges and a theme, and a theme names how its formations are made (`formations`: icicles, dripstone, chandelier, beams, columns, pillars, roots, or none) and may fix its dome (`dome`), so a stack is written and changed without code. A stack may say it holds only for a ground that rose (`over: risen`), because the default world's root carries the wild tag as its risen wild grounds do. The stacks: under the city culverts with steel pipes, then cisterns with brick piers standing in water, then slag; under the backrooms damp carpet and ceiling tile among concrete pillars, then flooded, then a boiler room; under the vaults a crypt of marble columns, then a reliquary with a glass chandelier, then drowned halls; under risen wild ground roots hanging from the roof over mushroom stalks, then fungus. The default world's root and the caves world keep frost, glow, gypsum, crystal and ember, and so does any ground with no stack (the two default stacks, `open` and `sealed`). The step that only moved the old themes into two default stacks left both digest lines of the planet, the caves, the city, the backrooms and the vaults as they were. Laws: `structure/citycaves`, `structure/backroomscaves`, `structure/vaultscaves` (the stack's own material lies in the chamber a well passes, and none of what an open cave has at that depth). Views down the wells: `cityculvert`, `cityslag`, `backroomsdamp`, `backroomsflooded`, `backroomsboiler`, `vaultscave`, `vaultsreliquary`, `vaultsdrowned`; `citywellchamber` now shows the cistern. Rendered and looked at: the culvert (badly framed, half of it shaft wall), the cistern, the damp level, the crypt. Not rendered: the other five, and anything under risen wild ground, which no script can find yet.
- **Checked on the tree with all three:** 158 tests, 201 views, 9 films, `content-check` 0 problems, `check-repaint` `differing=0` on all ten worlds, `check-forget` on lattice, all three programs built. The planet's lines: `digest populated=10140 materials 577ff9147db34183dd87b0399ea7cee0 occupancy 4795fce6dea3bc3499dc73afb53422a9` and `deep populated=856 materials 13b20451afe50eaf7b9a791739de39c0 occupancy 6b70f72354e30317fee4b0382c11cb5a`; the caves' `792299318bfa1c46c627074acb22b8ff`/`de12917b3f82640e251209faf00f7b06` and `044beebb090db151032d0da03c740d35`/`492634d04167f9edda773ec7f1e19179`; the first lines of the city `b93089f8301bf425dc9daa579f4c8910`/`327aad4928a3fe49b5d0594c16f9b694`, the backrooms `65e4cab5e5bc68e8314cbc16c5f834e3`/`8da571266e9b18a41d4159de6701b470` and the vaults `03bd7d21e0224dee1e7f115e3b9a6968`/`b6f0b867ffdd0020498e9d1c25bf2add`, their `deep` lines as before the stacks.
- **Speed.** `bench-gen` and `bench-drive` on the planet, `bench-gen` on the caves world and the four `live/` walls were taken three times before the day's work and three times after: none is slower. The stacks were measured against the same build with the stacks taken out of the content, three runs each way: `bench-gen` on the city, the backrooms and the vaults, and the planet's load and flight in `caapi --bench-load`, none slower with them. The load itself read slower in the afternoon than in the morning with the stacks in or out; that is taken to be the machine (the headless benches of the same code did not move), and it was not settled against the morning's binary, which was not kept.
- From the owner, on how to go on: "keep the .md files updated and of high quality", "keep going as far as you can. Assume answers and if it is wrong write it in a way that can be swapped out. That's how the codebase should be anyway", and "the focus is on gameplay and code quality from a high-level perspective. That if you make the code good and able to be inspected the game falls out of that". Reading: an open question no longer stops a package. The builder takes the answer this file already leans to, says so here as an **Assumed** line, and puts what was assumed where it can be changed without code (content, settings) or behind one function. Work is chosen for what a player can do and for whether the code and the world can be looked into.

- **Built the same day, under that instruction: rumors (J3).** A new way to notice an insight, by reading: `noticed-reading -> item | group` on an `insight` (`Trigger.read` in `crafting.d`). `game:pamphlet` is a block; using one learns one of the rumors that name it, chosen by a hash of where it lies (`rumorAt` in `game.d`), and a pamphlet whose rumor is known says its title again. The HUD offers "read" on it (`AimKind.readable`). `content-check` counts a read insight as reachable when a world places what it is read from. The library lays a pamphlet on the table of its reading rooms, meeting rooms and offices (`library:pamphlet` in `library.content`). Nine rumors: five in `game.content` (`rumor-quarter-turn`, `rumor-panels`, `rumor-bindings`, `rumor-ores`, `rumor-charcoal`) and four in `pharmacology.content` (fern and thornbush, ash and sulfur, tide, ember). The journal already keeps what is noticed by name, so the save did not change. Law: `structure/libraryrumor` (one reading adds one entry, a second adds none, no screen opens). **Assumed:** the nine sentences themselves, and that a rumor is a plain true statement and not a riddle; they are the `notice` texts of the `rumor-*` insights and nothing else reads them. **Assumed:** a pamphlet can be broken and carried off like any block, and read wherever it is put down (its rumor then follows its new place). The rest of J, the same day: the shape set on the catalog desk is kept in pharmacology's save section, after the queued work (`saved.d`); a save from before the change has no shape there and the desk opens empty. Law: `save/round-trip` ("A save keeps the shape set on the catalog desk"), which fails without the change. Doorway marks are closed, not built: they were one of the scout's three ways to make the catalog a walk, and the desk was built in their place. Also built the same day: the staff room has its own floor, ceiling and a band of wallpaper over the lockers (`staff-floor`, `staff-ceiling`, `staff-wall` on `library:materials`: concrete, ceiling tile, wallpaper; **Assumed**), so it reads as the employees' break room the owner described and not as one more reading room. The floor and ceiling replace what was painted there, and the wallpaper is one brush per staff quarter: library `bench-gen` parameters 63.3 to 63.6 ms against 64.0 to 65.2 before, three runs each, brushes 112066 against 112037. Library repaint differs nowhere, its ten tests pass; the library's lines after it are `digest populated=10140 materials 6f0437117d39dc28b79c6ba492eb361f occupancy 4bb933cdb3212bfd9cdf8fe4de5f60ed` and `deep ... materials 3af3a732562e078e48b9a6195c1244df occupancy aab9861bc186c468aa2bc7b586ddc2e1` (the deep line as before). View `librarystaffroom`, rendered: concrete floor, pale ceiling, yellow wallpaper over the grey lockers, the counter and its sink beyond, the bookshelves of the outer wall.
- **Tide and ember have keys** (`ideas.md`, In). A `signal-drive` may name a second signal with `also` and then follows the lower of the two (`SignalDrive.heard` in `drives.d`). Two drives in `pharmacology.content`: `tide-shows` (mist, gain -2) and `ember-shows` (mist and also lift, gain 1). Laws on the starter doses and the signaling: `biology/tide` (the test dose that opens sway takes tide's level to 1.1 and mist's to -1.1), `biology/rebound` (nine mist doses twenty seconds apart never show tide, and after they stop its level reaches 0.77, highest 160 seconds on), `biology/ember` (the mist test dose shows mist and leaves ember under the line; with the first trigger driven at 0.4 mist passes and ember does not, driven fully ember passes). Laws on what a player can pick, each eating one of nine wild strains at spawn into a fresh body: `biology/wild-mist` (some ferns and some flowers show mist), `biology/wild-ember` (some flowers show ember, no fern alone does), `biology/wild-tide` (some mushroom caps show tide, no thornbush, moss or fern does). The ember gain was 1.5 at first; measured on wild plants, every fern that showed mist showed ember as well, so ember was no harder to see than mist, and at 1 it takes the flower, which opens both triggers. The first measurements of the wild plants were wrong in another way: the script dosed one body strain after strain, and the tolerance it built up hid most of what the plants do; each strain now starts from `Game.startOver`. **Assumed:** the two gains and that tide reads the mist signal alone; they are the two content nodes. **Found false:** the idea in `ideas.md` that thornbush is a tide key; it fills the mist's lock without turning it, and eaten alone never takes mist far enough below rest (its best tide level was 0.04 against 0.6). The two rumors about tide and ember say what the laws show (mushrooms and the long mist; the flower). Nothing new was placed in the world. What the keys show was already there (read in `caves.content`): tide stone veins the cistern under the city, the drowned halls under the vaults and the sealed glow layer, and ember stone the slag under the city and both ember layers.
- **Seeing is noticing** (`ideas.md`, Crafting and learning: "a dose that revealed something"). An insight can name a hidden family with `noticed-revealing` (`Trigger.revealed`); the first time that family shows, the game notices it (`noticeRevealing`, called where the reveal note is posted in `settleReveal`). `content-check` counts such an insight reachable when some `signal-drive` drives that family. Three entries in `pharmacology.content`: `seen-mist`, `seen-tide`, `seen-ember`. Law: in `biology/tide`, the dose that shows tide adds the entry about the tide. **Assumed:** their wording. The journal view now holds a sighting and a rumor; rendered, the panel scrolls (the fourth entry runs past the fold), which with nineteen possible entries is most of it. Not built: listing the newest first, so a fresh entry is not in the middle of the list.
- **The caves under risen wild ground are still not seen.** A view was written that finds a wild ground rising from spawn (`ensureRisen` in `views.d`, the library's search made general) and looks one edge under its foot for the rooted layer's moss or mushroom stalks, with `ensureNearest` out to six hops. It found neither and was taken out. That search only hops sideways at one depth and reads 0.6 units around each hop, and caves under a risen ground exist only below its sinkholes, so a miss proves nothing. `material-census` on the `risenwild` rig reaches 2.5 units around the root and showed no cave material at all, which proves nothing either. What would settle it is a search for a sinkhole on a given risen ground (as `ensureLastFlat` finds the root's), or a census that can be pointed under one. Until then the `wild-stack` with `over: risen` is **Read** in the code and in its content, never **Tested**.
- **Abstraction work the same day** (details in `abstraction.md`): tiles take typed data (`typedTile` in `plugins/api/api.d`) everywhere but the caves', lattice's and planet's edge tiles; a world may register several edge tiles (no ABI change; law `structure/twoedges`); `Turtle.rollChance(percent)` became `rollPercent`, so it no longer shares a name with the backrooms' `rollChance(frame, odds)`; `carve.d` takes its angle constants from `turtle.d`; tile tasks remember `probeCrust` and `probeRelief` (about 2 to 3 percent off planet `bench-gen`, same-hour runs with it off and on); the default world's built and hollow grounds are content (`planet:grounds`), and so are its void layers (`planet:voids`). Every digest compared came out as before.
- **Notes crowd each other out.** The HUD keeps four notes (`MOST_NOTES`), newest last. Eating a flower that shows a family now posts the reveal and the journal entry, and with ember two of each, so in `plant/eat` a refusal note ("hold something living to eat it") was pushed out within the 1.5 s of the bite and the test failed. The test now reads the refusal one step after the bite. In play the same thing hides a note a player may need. Not changed: whether notes should merge, last longer, or keep refusals apart is a question of how the HUD should read.
- **The garden grows.** Rendered, the garden was a grey plain under a navy sky with white and red slabs on it and nothing living: it did not read as a garden. A quarter of a garden face that holds no form now rolls a bed (`bed` in `plugins/skies/plugin.d`): a kerb of the palette's step stone with a square of one plant inside, the plant picked by hash from `beds` on `skies:materials` (flower, fern, moss, thornbush). The plants are the wild plants of the body's chemistry, so a garden (and every garden that rises from the default world) is a place to gather them with that place's genes. Speed: beds added to the forms made the garden world's `bench-gen` slower (brushes 2813 to 3215), so the forms were traded down: `form-share` 0.6 to 0.4, `bed-share` 0.5 of the rest. Measured in the same hour against the old shares (which reproduce the old garden's digest exactly), three runs each: parameters 7.1, 7.6 and 7.1 ms before, 7.4, 7.1 and 7.2 after, brushes 2813 before and 2703 after. The planet's two digest lines did not move. View `gardenbeds`, rendered: a pink flowerbed and a green bed in kerbs between the red slabs and white monoliths. **Assumed:** the four plants and the shares; they are `beds` in `skies.content` and two lines of `plugins/skies/settings.conf`.
- **The cave stack views frame the chamber.** Each of them stood 0.3 behind the well's middle facing it, so the stair's core filled the middle of the frame, and turning round put the camera against the shaft wall. They now go down the well, find the layer's own floor nearest the chamber (wet concrete, lava, damp carpet, water, red sandstone) and stand back from it (`inStack` in `views.d`); `downWell` takes a yaw. Rendered and looked at, all seven: the culvert's pale floor running off down a passage, the slag's lava under grey rock, damp carpet along a wall, water against stained wallpaper, the boiler's lava room, the reliquary's red sandstone between marble, a red sandstone column standing in the drowned hall's water. `vaultscave` keeps the old frame (the crypt's floor is marble, and so is the vaults' well stair, so the nearest marble is the stair). Still not looked at: anything under risen wild ground.
- **`material-census` and `surface-census` follow `--worldgen`.** They counted the terrain world whatever was asked, so "is this block anywhere in the library" could not be answered from the tool; found while a test could not find a pamphlet.

### The engine as an engine

From the owner: "I want the source/ to be basically an engine and the real complexity come from the plugins." How the code is split is in `README.md` and `CLAUDE.md`. What the engine and the base mod still hold that belongs to another mod, each with what it is to become:

- **The terrain's tuning numbers.** Built on 2026-11-14: the seven climate processes' keep and spread, the mountain height and threshold, the lapse rate, the water level, the cover and soil depths, the rock band depths, the class limits, the tree and plant densities and the vein odds, fill and size are fields of the `terrain` node (required by the schema in `palette.d`, given in `base.content`), read once into `palette.shape`. The salts stay in the code, and so does the underside's swing, which is the crust's geometry. The bounds the burial proofs use are computed from the shape (`reliefBound`, `reliefSlack`, `shieldSlack`). Both digest lines of the caves world and of the library came out the same as before.
- **Names under `base:` that another mod owns.** Built on 2026-11-14: every name the game and pharmacology content define (wares, stations, groups, upgrades, insights, recipes, syntheses, dose routes, the starter body and its parts, chemistries, drives) carries its owner's prefix, and the eight machine blocks are defined, colour and all, in their owners' content (`game:workbench`, `furnace`, `anvil`, `beacon`; `pharmacology:press`, `lab`, `bench`, `collector`); `base.content` holds only what the base mod owns. The three roles whose names the blocks now take became `game:beacon-role`, `pharmacology:collector-role` and `pharmacology:bench-role`. The box's and the library's content and every script follow. A script that names something missing now says which name (`reportMissing` in `app/script.d`). Old saves were already refused by plugin ABI 35. What was there before: the wares, stations, groups, upgrades, insights and recipes in `game.content` and `pharmacology.content`, and the machine blocks (workbench, furnace, anvil, beacon, press, lab, bench, collector) whose colors are in `base.content`. Each takes its owner's prefix and the blocks move to their owner's content; every script in `app/scripts/` that names one changes with it.
